# Open work, in order

**Assembled 2026-10-22, after the day that closed the last hardware-blocked
row.** This is the single ordered list the repository did not have: `READINESS`
carries the release order and the engineering queue, `README` §9 carries the
detail, and the research questions were in neither. Three stale pointers were
found on 2026-10-22 alone — `READINESS`'s step 3, `README`'s *scale beyond four
nodes* cell, and the dangling item 9 → item 22 reference — each because a
dependency closed and the thing waiting on it was not re-read. **This file
exists to be the thing that gets re-read.**

**Every line carries what it is blocked on.** That is the only property that
makes rot detectable: a line whose blocker has gone is a line to act on, and a
line whose blocker cannot be stated was never a task.

**Who can do what**, used as a column below rather than repeated in prose:
**[M]** model, bounded; **[H]** needs the human's hands or machines;
**[D]** a decision only the human can take; **[X]** outward-facing or
third-party.

### 6.17 IS WORTH READING FOR HOW IT WAS MISSED, NOT FOR WHAT IT WAS

**THE HARNESS CERTIFIED THE DEFECT FOR AS LONG AS IT EXISTED.**
`worldline-ui.mjs` §4 asserted ``sample_period opens the window's real
records`` with the predicate ``/\d+ record/.test(sampleText)`` — a label
naming the missing feature, over a test that only required a digit beside the
word *record*. It was green on a runner throughout. **That is the
mislabelled-check class in its worst costume, and it is why this reached a
participant instead of CI.** When a check's label describes a capability,
assert the capability.

**A SECOND DEFECT CAME FROM READING THE OUTPUT AFTER IT EXITED 0.** A
`CapGrant` sits on every chain that has authorized signing credentials, and its
`entry_type` is the plain STRING `"CapGrant"` rather than an `{App:…}` object.
The first build assumed the object, so the first row of the first window told
the agent their own history could not be read — and the new check passed it,
because *"Entry type (unreadable)"* is a non-empty string. Machinery is now
named in plain words and still shown: Invariant 1 keeps raw history open, and
hiding rows from an agent's view of their own chain would be this UI deciding
what their history contains.

**NEW GATE: `scripts/check-entry-index-map.mjs`.** The client turns an action's
`entry_index` into a word a reader sees, and that index is POSITION-INDEXED
into the integrity zome's `EntryTypes` — the hazard `LinkTypes`' own comment
forbids, which nothing enforced for entry types and nothing connected to the
TypeScript map. Watched failing: one variant inserted mid-enum mislabels **ten**
indices, exit 1.

**AND THE COUNTER READS COMMENTS AS CODE.** `check-harness-counts.mjs` matches
a labelled call by regex, so a commented-out example counts as a call site —
then the comment written to explain *that* contained one and was counted
itself. Stated count, call sites and executed checks now all agree at 25. Do
not put the literal call shape in a comment.

### SIX DECISIONS WAITING ON THE AUTHOR, AND NONE OF THEM IS WORK

- **1.29** — per harness: register it, or declare it manual with the reason.
- **6.14's validation gap.** `validate_retraction` **permits a retraction
  naming itself as its own replacement** — it checks only that a named
  `replacement_claim` exists, not that it differs from the target. **MEASURED,
  not inferred:** removing the client guard turned 2 checks red and the second
  was *"nothing permanent was written"*, so the conductor accepted the loop
  onto a real chain. Closing it is an integrity change and a `protocol_version`
  bump.
- **3.2's asymmetry.** `get_standing` takes the strict reading (`None => true`,
  nothing dies); `get_effective_conductance` applies **thirty days regardless**.
  SPEC §7's own note already admits that half survives. Either conductance
  takes the strict reading — changing what every existing conductance read
  returns, explicitly not taken on 2026-10-19 — or the asymmetry is recorded
  as deliberate.
- **6.17's `[D]` — should `sample_period` honour `domain_tag`?** Measured: it
  filters the chain on TIME ALONE, so two boundaries sharing a window return
  byte-identical results matching neither's `entry_count`. That is arguably
  the point — the chain's raw answer is what makes it a CHECK on the trace
  rather than a repeat of it — so the coordinator was left alone and the
  window explains the difference on screen. Changing it needs no DNA bump,
  which makes it cheap enough to be worth deciding deliberately rather than
  by default.
- **6.19's `[D]` — where does a reply to a critique appear?** The write is
  three fields and the read already exists (`get_critiques_for` takes any
  hash; `loadCritiqueTree` already walks it to depth 3). What needs deciding
  is the flat list's shape: it is in the order the DHT returned it and a
  nested reply has no place in that order, so indenting it is a client
  deciding a thread's structure. **6.14's `[D]` on entry order is the same
  question one row over**, and this one is owed rather than merely open — a
  reply sent 2026-11-08 told the pilot's first publisher that an author can
  defend by critiquing a critique, which is true of the protocol and not of
  the app.
- **7.18 / 7.19** — both written; publishing is a decision.

### THREE OPERATIONAL TRAPS THIS SESSION PAID FOR

- **`scripts/check-*.mjs` IS NOT THE GATE SET, and it cost a red CI run.** The
  validation corpus stores the **line number** of every `Invalid` site, so a
  twelve-line comment in the integrity zome shifted **97** of them and
  reddened `no-conductor` for no behavioural reason. **Any edit to
  `dna/integrity/src/lib.rs` — comments included — needs
  `node scripts/validation-corpus/enumerate.mjs` re-run.** The full
  no-conductor node list is in the session memory note; it is fourteen scripts,
  not the `check-*` glob.
- **`partition-rejoin` fails in CI roughly a quarter of the time**, and
  presents as a **SETUP failure** rather than an assertion failure — phase 0 is
  the baseline *before* any partition. README already records non-crossings at
  **27%**. Confirm the diff cannot reach `partition-rejoin.mjs` or
  `network.sh`, re-run once, and treat a second failure as signal. Note
  `gh pr checks` reports a check's **last concluded** status, so it keeps
  showing `fail` while a re-run is in flight — trust
  `gh run view <id> --json status,conclusion`. The *never re-run `--failed`*
  rule is **release-scoped**, not general.
- **`pkill -f "holochain --"` KILLS THE ALWAYS-ON PILOT NODE.** Done on
  2026-11-10 while clearing an orphaned sandbox holding port 8889; the sandbox
  conductor and the pilot node are the same binary. systemd restarted it
  (`NRestarts=1`) and it was verified healthy, but the orphan only existed
  because `sandbox.sh clean` and `start` were fired in overlapping background
  calls. Run them sequentially, wait for *are up*, and kill by the PID holding
  the port — never by process name.

### TWO MORE TRAPS, BOTH OF WHICH PRODUCED A GREEN THAT MEANT NOTHING

- **`npm run build` IS `tsc && vite build`, AND `noUnusedLocals` IS ON.** An
  injection that leaves a local unused fails the typecheck, never reaches
  `vite build`, and leaves the PREVIOUS bundle in `dist/` for the harness to
  drive. Injection G of `reading-a-thread.mjs` reported ALL CHECKS PASSED
  **twice** that way — once on `cap`, then again on `PROSE_BOX_CAP` after
  `cap` was deleted — and the only tell was a 200-line draft reporting the
  cap's exact value. **Check for `built in` before trusting a run**, and
  prefer an injection that neutralises a value over one that removes it.
- **A FIXTURE THAT SPENDS CRITIQUES HAS A RUNS-PER-HOUR CEILING.** The
  integrity zome allows 20 critiques per agent per 3600s;
  `reading-a-thread.mjs` spends 2 of the stranger's per run, so about **ten
  runs against one sandbox**. Two injections first reported 0 red because the
  conductor refused the SETUP — the harness printed the zome's own refusal
  and exited non-zero, which is the right failure, and both were re-run
  against a clean sandbox for new agent keys.

### THE STANDING LESSON HELD, AND THREE FINDINGS CAME FROM BEING WRONG

For any check of the form *X is NOT shown*, ask what makes it capable of
failing and put the control that answers it FIRST. This session: a harness
caught **6.13's own defect in brand-new code** (an unasked read rendered as
*"nothing came back"*, visible only to a MutationObserver — every check that
READ the panel was green); a control **overturned a written conclusion about
`get_standing`** before it became a result; and a self-review found **a check
of mine whose cheaper disjunct could not fail** (`|| back.length >= 2`). An
injection that PASSES is a finding about the assertion, not a gap — #455's
cross-group injection stayed green because the refusal has two independent
layers.

**HOW TO READ A SOURCE CHAIN WITHOUT WRITING TO IT, because the obvious route
is a write.** `get_agent_constitution` is a zome call and needs signing
credentials, and authorizing those **commits a capability grant** to a chain
where nothing can ever be deleted. Use `admin.dumpFullState` instead — purely
read-only. Admin ws is `127.0.0.1:33135` origin `kangaroo`, app ws `39001`
origin `webhapp://webhappwindow`; the client is in
`mobile-ui/node_modules/@holochain/client`. Entries are msgpack; Constitution
is `entry_index` 6, Membrane 5, Claim 1.

**THREE DEFECTS FOUND BY VERIFYING THE RELEASE, NONE OF WHICH ANY GREEN JOB
WOULD HAVE SHOWN** — the pattern this file keeps recording, holding again:

- **`latest-mac.yml` advertised ONE architecture on every release since
  `v0.1.0`.** `scripts/merge-mac-yamls.mjs` had `holochain/launcher`
  hardcoded, inherited from the template, so it asked the Launcher whether it
  had a `v0.3.1`, logged `No release found. Skipping merge` and exited 0.
  Harmless only because every prior release was a MINOR, which
  electron-updater refuses to offer across — **`v0.3.1` is the first
  auto-update-eligible release this app has cut**, so it would have handed an
  Apple Silicon install the Intel build. Fixed, with a stale-snapshot race in
  the same script that meant the merge only ever worked because the two mac
  jobs happened to run minutes apart.
- **Re-releasing an existing tag no longer leaves assets stale — on macOS it
  FAILS.** electron-builder now overwrites, but `productName` contains a SPACE
  and GitHub stores the blockmap with a DOT, so its delete misses and the
  upload 422s. Fix: delete the two `*.zip.blockmap` assets and re-run the two
  mac jobs. **And every re-push replaces every artifact — the builds are not
  reproducible, so any verification done before a re-push is void.**
- **`INSTALL.md` and the invitation §1 still said `v0.3.0` — the THIRD
  occurrence of a failure that file already documented twice.** It mattered:
  §1 asks every invitee for a published `Constitution`, and on `v0.3.0` that
  ask is **unexecutable**, because before 6.7 the only route to
  `publish_constitution` was founding your own domain. **Cutting a release is
  not finished until those two files name the new version.**

**A PROCESS FAILURE WORTH THE SAME TREATMENT, because it was not the code.**
`docs/funding/holo-ventures-approach.md` — an unsent approach to a named
funder — was swept into a commit by a blanket `git add -A` meant for two other
files, and reached public `main` for about thirty minutes before #381 removed
it. It remains in history at `548031f`; a purge was judged not worth a
force-push of a public repository. **The rule: never `git add -A` in a
repository holding untracked private drafts**, which this one now does.

**WHAT IS ACTUALLY LEFT, so it is not re-derived.** **7.14's `[M]` half — make
the app REMEMBER a bootstrap override — is the only unblocked bounded work**,

behind it; **7.2**'s procurement; and §6's four screens, still one decision

**ALSO DONE, AND IT WILL NOT RECUR:** all four release tags in

**So the standing instruction is stronger than "check the list".** A closed
row is not a closed claim: the list tracks work, and the prose that quotes the
work's result rots silently because nothing gates it. After anything ships,
grep for the fact it falsified — here, `v0.2.0`, `protocol_version: 9` and
*"two forks behind"* — before concluding there is nothing bounded to do.
`READINESS.md` was swept on the day and is the model for what that looks like.

**Research opened rather than closed:** 3.7 states the sybil question once,
with its boundary written down — *the cheapest promise structure that makes
sybil cost high enough without creating a centre* — and fences off the version
that cannot be answered, since validation can no more observe a person than it
can read a clock. Candidate (1), attestation capacity, is already shipped.

**THE COLD-READ INSTRUCTION BELOW EARNED ITS KEEP ON THE FIRST TRY.** #333 was
found open because a required check had gone red — `ui`, `taxonomy-ui` §5 —
not because the queue had stalled. Check that first, every time. And note what
the red turned out to be worth: chasing it produced item 1.7 and overturned a
documented cause (see the contention entry below).

**Each of the five was branched before the one ahead of it landed**, so
`strict: true` left each `BEHIND` in turn and each was updated from `main`
rather than moving the rule. Five sequential ~20-minute cycles for five
documentation changes is the concrete cost of that rule, and it is the evidence
the `[D]` below is asking for.

Before those two this section said **nothing**, and had listed five items on
2026-10-22; all five merged, and so did everything they were waiting on. The
heading is kept rather than deleted because an empty in-flight list is itself
the status.

`main` has branch protection with **`strict: true`**, and on 2026-10-23 that
cost was paid in full: fourteen pull requests landed one at a time, each merge
putting the rest behind and forcing a fresh ~20-minute cycle. It was relaxed
for the nine documentation PRs — verified safe first by building the combined
tree locally and running all eight drift checkers against it — and **restored
immediately after**. `enforce_admins` is true, so there is no lighter
alternative: `--admin` does not bypass it, the rule itself has to move.

~~**Whether `strict` earns its keep on a repository where most pull requests
touch only Markdown remains a decision [D]**~~ — **DECIDED 2026-10-25:
`strict: true` STAYS, AND THE COST WAS IN THE WRONG PLACE.** The bill was read
as the price of the rule, and it is not. It is the price of **what the rule is
bound to**: five required contexts, none of them path-filtered, four of which
cannot be affected by a Markdown-only diff and all of which ran anyway. #347
is the receipt — a five-document change, nothing else, and it spent `conductor`
11m59s and `ui` 12m37s proving that sentences about `v0.2.0` had not broken a
real DHT.

**`strict` itself earns its keep, and there is a demonstrated catch rather than
an argument.** Two pull requests each inserting into
`dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs` regenerate the validation inventory
independently, are green alone, and red `main` when the second lands carrying
the first's line numbers. That is exactly the semantic conflict `strict` exists
to prevent, and it went red in CI on 2026-10-24 for that reason. Dropping the
rule would trade a proven catch for throughput.

**So the binding moved instead.** `conductor`, `network`, `ui` and `zomes` now
carry a `scope` job that skips them when the diff is documentation and nothing
else; `live-verify` is deliberately ungated, because every checker that reads a
document runs there. A docs-only pull request satisfies all five contexts in
about ninety seconds, and `strict: true` keeps its teeth on everything that
touches `dna/` or `scripts/`. **It is a conditional and not a `paths:`
filter**, which is not a detail: a workflow skipped by `paths:` creates no
check run, and a required context with no check run hangs the pull request on
*"Expected — waiting for status"* forever, while a job skipped by a conditional
reports success. The documented remedy — a stub workflow sharing the job name —
is unsound here, because `paths` and `paths-ignore` are not complements and a
mixed diff would have both jobs reporting under one name.

**The new risk is a green that means nothing**, which is worse than the red
that means nothing this repository already refuses: a classifier that calls a
code diff documentation would have four required checks pass having run
nothing. Three things answer it rather than one — the classifier is
**default-deny** (anything not root-level `*.md` or `docs/**.md` is code, so a
mistake is a slow pull request and never an unverified merge), the gate
expression carries `!cancelled()` (without it a failed `scope` job would skip
its dependents into success), and `check-ci-scope.mjs` asserts all of it from
inside the one workflow the gate cannot touch — including that no harness any
gated job runs reads a document. See `scripts/ci-scope.mjs`.

**AND THE CONTENTION EXPLANATION IS WRONG — the mechanism cannot exist on this
CI.** The 2026-10-23 correlation stands as an observation: six branches at
once, four `ui` failures, every one `taxonomy-ui` §5 — *"proposing a species
from the UI"* — timing out on `waitForSelector`, against 4-for-4 green when the
same branches ran solo. But every workflow is `runs-on: ubuntu-latest`
(`.github/workflows/ui.yml:160` and its six siblings), so **each job gets its
own VM**: jobs share no CPU and no ports, since `sandbox.sh` binds 8887-8889 on
`127.0.0.1` inside the runner it is given. And on 2026-10-24 #333's `ui` failed
on the same selector at 04:17:38, **by which time every other job in both
batches had finished except its own `conductor`** — one sibling, on another
machine — then passed on re-run with the tree unchanged.

~~So two things are now established and one is not. **Established**: §5 has an
intermittent race that does not need a loaded queue, and a re-run clears it.
**Not established**: why six branches correlated with four failures at all.~~

**ANSWERED THE SAME DAY BY #340, AND BRANCH COUNT PLAYED NO PART.**
`taxonomy-ui` §5 was missing the 3-second settle its own section 3 already
took. `render()` rebuilds `app.innerHTML` wholesale, so a re-render landing
between filling the species name and clicking **Propose** destroyed the input;
the form submitted an empty name; validation correctly refused it
(*"CritiqueSpecies name cannot be empty"*). Nothing was written, so nothing was
readable, so the wait timed out.

**The race is per-run and decided by the runner's speed.** A development machine
wins it every time — which is why this harness passed locally all day, twice
against the unfixed code — and a runner carrying vite, Playwright, lair and a
conductor on four cores does not. Six branches meant six samples of a coin a
slow runner loses; four reds in six against none in four solo is **sampling, not
causation**.

Two sentences above are therefore struck rather than edited. *"A re-run clears
it"* was also wrong: the re-run on #339 failed identically, twice in a row,
which is what ended the flake theory. And **"push one branch at a time" is dead
as a remedy** — it addresses a variable with no causal role. The thing that
actually settled it was making the read declare its own mode (§7b.2, item 1.7).

**THREE ATTRIBUTIONS WERE WRONG IN SEQUENCE, and that is the lesson worth
keeping.** Inter-job contention — mechanically impossible, every job holds its
own VM. Then the render path — which *this very section* named as the suspect.
Then commit-to-readable latency — the instrumentation's own first conclusion,
which conflated a refused write with a slow one. Each was retired by a
measurement rather than by an argument, and that is the only reason to trust the
fourth.

---

## 1. Immediate engineering — bounded, and the model can do all of it

| # | item | who | blocked on |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1.1 | ~~**Why `send_remote_signal` does not deliver.**~~ Stage 1's receiving half, cost accounting, idempotence and control all verify; remote delivery does not. Every failure path in the host's implementation is `tracing::info!` then `return` under *"timeouts and errors are ignored"*, so the default log level hides it. Diagnostic: both nodes at `RUST_LOG=info` **CLOSED 2026-10-23 (#318). It delivers and always did** — the harness unwrapper missed `value.payload.PeerSignal` by one level, so *"nothing arrived"* and *"something arrived I cannot read"* were the same observation. `RUST_LOG` could not have found it: `network.sh:592` exports `CUSTOM_FILTER` at base `warn`, which overrides it. `NETAUDIT=trace` did. **Six silent paths, not the four the HDK documents.** | — | done |
| 1.2 | ~~**The scale run above twelve.**~~ `scale-dht.mjs` exists, `EPI_SCALE_NODES` parameterises it, twelve all claimed a FULL arc. The figure wanted is **the N at which an arc first narrows**. ~95-98 MB and ~8% of a core per conductor, so 48 is ~4.7 GB and ~4 cores against 11 GB and 12 cores free **CLOSED 2026-10-23 (#323). Measured at 48 members: 48 of 48 arcs still FULL**, 5,983 MB across 49 conductors, load 3.1-5.6 of 12 cores. **There is no N** — `kitsune2_gossip`'s narrowing path sits behind a `sharding` cargo feature that is OFF, and holochain declares `unstable-sharding = []`, an empty feature that does not forward to it. A feature-flag question, not a scale question. | — | done |
| 1.3 | ~~**The `ui` flake, which is probably not flake.**~~ Four failures on 2026-10-22 across `taxonomy-ui` and `graph-ui`, every one a `waitForSelector` timeout of the shape *write something, then wait for it to render*, every one green on re-run. The two-house run established that transit is sub-second and **local integration under contention** is the cost, which is exactly what a loaded runner has. Wanted: a counted record beside the harnesses the way the `network` pass rate is kept, and the wait declaring its own mode per §7b.2 **COUNTED 2026-10-23 (#316). CAUSE CORRECTED 2026-10-24 — see §0 and 1.7:** the 4-for-4-solo against 4-for-4-batch correlation stands as an observation, but *inter-job* contention cannot be its mechanism — every workflow is `runs-on: ubuntu-latest`, so no two jobs share a machine. What looked like it survived was `scripts/live-verify/README.md`'s *intra*-job hypothesis — one runner contending with itself — and **that was wrong too, settled by #340 the same day**: §5 was missing the 3-second settle §3 already took, so a re-render wiped the form and validation refused an empty name. Runner speed decides the race, but it was never about op integration. Re-running does **not** reliably clear it either — #339's re-run failed identically. See §0 and 1.7. **A THIRD HARNESS JOINED THE SHAPE ON 2026-11-01, WHICH IS WHAT THIS ROW ASKED TO BE COUNTED: `foresight-ui`.** On PR #403 — a comment-only edit to `index-lens.mjs` and one documentation row, so nothing it could plausibly have caused — `ui` failed with `FAIL: the commitment appears in the pending list on screen`, `FAIL: and the commitment reached the conductor, question bound`, then `HARNESS ERROR: locator.click: Timeout 30000ms exceeded`. Same *write something, then wait for it to render* shape as the `taxonomy-ui` and `graph-ui` failures above, on a harness this row had never named. **It CLEARED on re-run (13m50s, green, unchanged tree), which is the half that does not match the record:** #339's re-run failed identically, and this one did not — so *re-running does not reliably clear it* is still true as written and is not true of every instance. The §5 settle that explained `taxonomy-ui` is a `taxonomy-ui` fix; whether `foresight-ui` has its own missing settle is **not established here** and nothing in this entry claims it. **A SECOND `foresight-ui` OBSERVATION, PR #405, 2026-11-01 — AND IT IS THE SAME THREE LINES.** `FAIL: the commitment appears in the pending list on screen`, `FAIL: and the commitment reached the conductor, question bound`, `HARNESS ERROR: locator.click: Timeout 30000ms exceeded` at `foresight-ui.mjs:409` — byte-for-byte the #403 failure, §7 then §8 cascading off it. **The diff again could not have caused it:** #405 changed three `scripts/*.mjs` endpoint callers and two docs, and **nothing under `scripts/live-verify/` or `.github/workflows/ui.yml` references any of the three** (the only mention anywhere is a comment in `index-lens.mjs`), so there is no code path from the change into the `ui` job. **It cleared on re-run again** — all seven checks green, with every other job passing on its FIRST attempt including `partition-rejoin`. So `foresight-ui` was at that point **twice observed and twice cleared by one re-run**, which looked like it sharpened the split this row recorded: the harnesses differing in whether a re-run clears them, with `foresight-ui` always on the clearing side where #339 was always on the other. **THAT READING IS WITHDRAWN — see the 2026-11-01 entry below, where it failed, cleared on re-run, and then failed AGAIN on the same pull request.** The clearing-side claim was built on two instances and did not survive the third and fourth. **Two instances is still not a mechanism** — and the detail that resists the settle-timing explanation is that §7's CHAIN-READ check failed alongside the render check, so the write did not land, not merely that it did not paint. Counted, not explained. Recorded as the counted observation this row wanted rather than as a cause. **A THIRD AND FOURTH OBSERVATION, 2026-11-01, on PRs #407 and #413** — again `foresight-ui`, again byte-for-byte the same three lines at `foresight-ui.mjs:409`, and again on diffs that **cannot reach the `ui` job**: both are documentation-only negative-evidence records (comments in `scripts/live-verify/*.mjs` plus one README table row). **So `foresight-ui` stands at four observations in two days, every one on a diff incapable of causing it.** That is no longer an intermittency noticed in passing — it is the most reproducible failure in this suite, and the §7 chain-read check failing beside the render check in every instance still says the WRITE does not land rather than the paint arriving late. **AND A CORRECTION TO THIS ROW'S OWN CLAIM, MADE THE SAME DAY IT WAS WRITTEN.** The earlier entries above said `foresight-ui` had been *twice cleared by one re-run* and was therefore *always on the clearing side* where #339 was always on the other. **#413 falsifies that on a single pull request:** its `ui` job failed, was re-run and went GREEN, and then failed AGAIN with the identical three lines after the branch was updated to current `main` — a content-identical rebase, no source change. So a re-run clearing `foresight-ui` does not predict the next run, and the clean split between it and #339 does not exist. **What survives is the weaker and more useful statement: a re-run SOMETIMES clears it, with no known predictor.** The claim was built on two instances, and the third and fourth broke it — the same two-observations-is-not-a-mechanism caution this row already prints, applied to itself. **THAT HYPOTHESIS WAS TESTED 2026-11-01 AND IT IS CONFIRMED, BY SOURCE READING RATHER THAN BY ANOTHER RUN. `foresight-ui` §7 HAS `taxonomy-ui` §5's DEFECT.** The chain: `main.ts`'s `render()` is `app.innerHTML = ''` plus a full rebuild (`main.ts:588`), and the commit button's handler reads `.value` off the input elements **its own closure captured**. So a render landing between the harness's `fill()` and its `click()` replaces both — the click hits a NEW button whose closure reads NEW EMPTY inputs, the handler's own guard refuses with *"A question, a prediction and a salt are all required"*, and **nothing is committed**. Observable: no pending row AND nothing on the chain, which is exactly the pair of reds, with §8 cascading off the absent pending item. **The renders are neither hypothetical nor the harness's doing:** `main.ts` has **twenty-one fire-and-forget `void load*()` calls and twelve of its fourteen loaders end in `render()`**, and §7 reaches the form FROM A CLAIM CARD, which fires `loadResonance`, `loadGroundingChain`, `loadClaimEpistemicState` and `loadFrictionStatus`. Any of those resolving mid-fill wipes the form. **This also explains both things the counting never could**: the intermittency is a race whose window is the gap between two Playwright calls, so a loaded runner loses it more often (the ~17% job rate, and §7b's contention finding), and a re-run clears it or not depending on timing alone — which is why the *clears on re-run* predictor had to be withdrawn. **§7 was the one interactive point in the file with NO settle**: §3 waits 6s, the preview 3s, §8 2s and 3s. **FIXED, and made self-diagnosing rather than merely fixed**: a 3s settle before the fill, the filled values READ BACK and asserted before the submit so a wiped form reports itself instead of masquerading as a commit that never happened, and the UI's own error box printed when the pending list fails to render. 25 checks become 26. **If it recurs now, the new check says which of the two it was** — and four observations produced no diagnosis precisely because nothing distinguished them. | — | done |
| 1.4 | ~~**Two desktop installs talking to each other.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-10-26 — IT CROSSED.** A `Claim` published on machine 1 into domain `xnet-1026-a` appeared on machine 2 **on its first `Load claims`**, between two packaged `v0.3.0` installs on two machines. **This is the first time two installed copies of this app have exchanged anything**; every prior crossing used `hc sandbox` conductors, which declare no network seed and so cannot see desktop peers at all, which is exactly why the packaging was the one layer no measurement had reached. Verified from the CELL rather than from release notes: machine 1's running DNA hashes to `uhC0kozxlYwR0REOn2iS4NswBOPzJ7I6kKsRV6o3WOEuoQOuEi-4o`, the documented `0.3.x` hash, read live through the admin interface; both installs reached `Conductor ready.` on dynamic admin ports (42253, 33239); and `INSTALL.md`'s two origins are confirmed — `kangaroo` on admin, `webhapp://webhappwindow` on the app interface. **Release step 3's *"against that build"* clause is closed, so 7.4's last blocker is gone.** **THE RUN FOUND TWO DOCUMENTATION DEFECTS, AND ONE WAS A CORRECTION THAT WAS ITSELF WRONG** — `READINESS.md` §1 and `INSTALL.md` both said `v0.3.0` logs `Connected to admin websocket.` *instead of* `Conductor ready.` and said *"do not grep for it"*; measured across all three installed profiles, `Conductor ready.` is in every one and the two replacement strings are in **none**, so the instruction would have reported a working conductor as dead. Also established: **founding a domain is not a prerequisite for publishing into one** (`validate_claim` requires only a non-empty `domain`), so the advice to found one first would have written a permanent `Constitution` for nothing. **AND THE REVERSE CROSSED TOO**, measured separately rather than assumed: a claim published on machine 2 into a fresh domain `xnet-1026-b` — which machine 1 had loaded and seen EMPTY first — appeared on machine 1. **Both directions between two packaged installs.** That matters because a one-way result is consistent with a receiver that can only receive, and the ten invitees will all be publishers. **It does not soften §3b**: both machines were up with the receiver attached before each write, which is the favourable case, so the five-to-twelve-minute returning-node catch-up is still the number to quote. Closes the clause still open in release step 3 — *"against that build"* — since both ends of every 2026-10-22 crossing were `hc sandbox` conductors. The relay path is measured; the packaging is not **NARROWED 2026-10-23 (#325): the protocol half is DONE and the blocker is smaller than this row said.** Verified against a running `v0.2.0` — the cell hashes to the seeded desktop hash recorded here, the zome reports `declared_version: 9`, a Claim written through the app's own conductor rendered in its UI, and a Retraction showed it retracted by its author while the claim stayed readable by hash. The conductor is reachable **without the GUI** (admin origin `kangaroo`, app interface `webhapp://webhappwindow`), so the crossing test is scriptable. **What remains: one Setup click per profile** — `passwordMode` is `password-optional` and the conductor does not launch until somebody clicks — **and a second machine.** ~~Note `v0.2.0` is `protocol_version: 9` against main's 11, so testing it validates packaging two forks behind; whether that is what *"against that build"* means is a decision.~~ **NOT A DECISION ANY MORE: `v0.3.0` shipped at 11, so run the test against `v0.3.0` and the clause means the documented build.** Three things do not carry over from the `v0.2.0` record — the seed is `Epistemic Resonance-0.3.x`, the running hash is `uhC0kozxl…`, and the launch log says `Connected to admin websocket.` rather than `Conductor ready.` See `INSTALL.md`. | H | **done 2026-10-26** |

| 1.6 | ~~**The parked gate.**~~ Branch `chore/gate-probabilistic-setup`, no commits, to gate probabilistic setup searches in `ordering-policy.mjs` (three sites, lines 244/266/310) **CLOSED 2026-10-23 (#317), then corrected again in #326.** The gate was right; its arithmetic was not. §2 compares 32 hashes against ONE pivot, so a miss is *"is the pivot the maximum of 33"* = **1/33 ≈ 3%**, not 2^-32 — and CI drew it while the gate printed the false figure one line above the red. Repaired with seven independent pivots: (1/33)^7 = 2^-35.3. **Four wrong odds in one file, three of them in the commits that fixed the others.** | — | done |

The suspect is the write-then-render path: `mobile-ui/src/main.ts:3382-3402` awaits `create_critique_species`, sets `proposingSpecies = false`, then `await loadTaxonomy()` — the card exists only after that refetch returns, while the harness's independent-client read on the next line would still pass, which localises it to the render half. Applied to the three selectors that have actually failed and no others — the suite has about a hundred waits and the discipline names a shape, not all of them. Both harnesses re-run green against a real conductor (`taxonomy-ui` 25, `graph-ui` 14). It also generalised `check-harness-counts.mjs`, whose module exclusion was a hardcoded `chromium.mjs` with a comment asserting it was the only non-harness there. **AND IT FOUND THE CAUSE IN TWO RUNS — #340, with the suspect named in this very row exonerated.** `main.ts:3382-3402`'s write-then-render path was innocent: the species read NOT READABLE at the moment of timeout, twice consecutively, so the screen was right to show nothing. The bug was in the HARNESS — §5 lacked the 3-second settle §3 already took, so a re-render wiped the species name, the form submitted empty, and validation refused it. `ui` is green in CI on the fix, which is the only evidence that counts here. **The module's own first conclusion was wrong too** and is corrected: it called "not readable" a latency finding, but a REFUSED write and a SLOW one are identical to a probe — this was a refusal, visible on screen throughout — so it now reads the page for a displayed error on that branch. | M | done |

Its own step-2 comment says that, and READINESS records it. **The consequence nobody drew**: a green `conductor` run means *the tarball we would publish from HEAD works against a real conductor*, and says nothing about what a stranger installing today gets. **THIS FAILURE CLASS HAS ALREADY HAPPENED** — the registry copy of `0.1.3` was broken from 2026-10-15 until `0.1.4` because a dependency RANGE resolved to a bad version, which no local-tarball test can see by construction. Wanted: a by-version install **from the registry** into an empty project, driven against a conductor, reporting which version it resolved so the check cannot quietly pass against a stale pin — and run on a schedule rather than per-push, since it tests the registry and not the diff. Not wired into any workflow — it tests the registry, which does not change when this repository does, so **scheduling it is a [D] and a cron line, not code**. | M | done |

| 1.9 | ~~**`check-packages.mjs` is gated by nothing, and the reason is a reflex rather than a decision.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-10-25.** It is now a `live-verify.yml` step, and the sentence that hid it is fixed. **THE ROW UNDERSTATED ITS OWN SURFACE, which is the second time in one day** — it said *"wiring it into `live-verify.yml` is the whole change — it needs no conductor, no DHT and no browser"*, all true and not the whole change: `check-packages.mjs` runs `npm run build` itself, so it needs both packages' dependency trees, and `mcp-server` must resolve the SDK from THIS checkout rather than the registry copy, which is broken on npm. So `conductor.yml`'s two build steps are now a marked shared block in both files, gated byte-for-byte by `check-workflow-setup-drift.mjs` — a drifted copy would make the publish check green about a package nobody ran. 4.5 did the same thing with a link type (#350). **Both were found by building the thing, not by re-reading the row.** It asks whether `npm publish` would produce a *working* package by reading what `npm pack --dry-run` actually puts in the tarball — `files` missing so `src/` ships, `dist` listed but never built, a `file:../thing` dependency that resolves here and nowhere else, a licence declared in metadata and absent from the tarball. None of those is visible until somebody installs the published package, by which point the version number is spent. `scripts/live-verify/README.md` records it as *"not a live-verify harness, but run by the same reflex"*, which was the accurate description when there was no CI at all. **There is CI now, and that passage is ALREADY STALE for its other example**: `check-harness-counts.mjs` is introduced the same way, two paragraphs down, and has been a step in `live-verify.yml` for weeks. **1.8 does not cover this**: `published-packages.mjs` drives the tarball the REGISTRY is serving, from `conductor.yml`; this one asks whether the local tree would publish something installable, which is the question a release asks and the one `v0.3.0` asked four times. Wiring it into `live-verify.yml` is the whole change — it needs no conductor, no DHT and no browser. **One thing it is NOT, recorded because it was discovered by trying**: the fix to that stale passage cannot ride a documentation-only pull request, because `scripts/live-verify/README.md` classifies as *code* under the scope gate — `check-harness-counts.mjs` reads it. That is the classifier being right rather than coarse. | [M] | **done 2026-10-25** |
| 1.10 | ~~**Ten validation rules the corpus demands nothing of.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-10-25 — the cases are written and both declarations are deleted.** Note what was NOT in the way: both link types have been `AttachmentLinkKind` variants since `protocol_version: 11`, so `attempt_false_attachment_link` reached all ten rules the whole time and `needs_prober` was correctly 0. **Nothing was blocked; the rules simply had no cases, and the corpus could not say so** because its per-type loop iterates `COVERED_LINKS`. The five rules of each type are a CHAIN — ActionHash, resolves, right type, base matches the entry's own target, author is the owner — so each owner case moves the base as well as the target, to agent 2's own target, or the base rule fires one line above the rule being probed and the case passes for the wrong reason. The original text follows. **Ten validation rules the corpus demands nothing of.** `TargetToAssessment` and `TargetToSettlementStatus` carry **five assertable rules each** and are absent from `replay.mjs`'s `COVERED_LINKS`, so the per-type coverage loop never looks at them: the corpus reports *"0 failed, 0 skipped"* and an `N of N` line for every type it is asked about, and it is not asked about these. Shipped that way at `protocol_version: 11` — settlement's three ENTRY types went into `COVERED` and its two LINK types did not go into `COVERED_LINKS` — and it stayed green for four days. **Found 2026-10-25 while measuring the corpus README's rotted figures, and the same omission was made again on the 4.5 branch the same day**, caught there only because its cases happened to name rules and tripped the orphan check from the other side. **The structural half is now closed**: `check-corpus-coverage.mjs` fails on any type with assertable rules that is neither covered nor declared, and both of these are declared with their reason pointing here, so the number stays visible. What remains is the work itself — ten link cases with their fixtures, in the shape 5a/5b/5c already are. | [M] | **done 2026-10-25** |
| 1.11 | ~~**Four counted claims about link types, and the two that were wrong in opposite directions.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-10-25 — corrected in five documents and gated in `check-spec-drift.mjs`.** SPEC §5.19 said *"**eighteen** link types MUST bind ... that is **every link type anything writes**"*. **The eighteen was right — it is §5.19's own table — and the clause after it was overtaken by three protocol versions**, `protocol_version: 11` alone adding two bound types; `READINESS.md` and `README.md` carried the same stale pair. Measured: **31 declared, 27 written, 27 with a `validate_create_link` branch, 23 bound, 4 unconditional.** **The error ran the other way too, and that one understated the protocol.** `scripts/validation-corpus/README.md` — swept the same day — listed `ClaimToBridgeRecord` as having *"no `validate_create_link` branch at all"*, telling a reader a validated link type was unvalidated. It has a branch, **seven** rules and a case. **The cause is worth more than the fix: the sweep read the INVENTORY's 26 as a fact about the zome.** `ClaimToBridgeRecord` shares one branch with `MewToBridgeRecord` (`else if A || B`) and `enumerate.mjs` files a branch under the first `LinkTypes::` its regex finds — so all seven rules sit under `MewToBridgeRecord`, which IS in `COVERED_LINKS`, so the per-type loop demands all seven and `replay.mjs` asserts all seven. **Nothing was untested; one type is counted under its sibling's name, and `replay.mjs` said so at its bridge spec the whole time.** A denominator borrowed from a tool is a claim about the tool. SPEC §5.17, which says four, was right and was not consulted. **WHAT IS NOW GATED is the set equality, not the integers**: `written === has-a-branch`, both directions, so a link type the coordinator writes with no rule fails CI — §5.17's *"each acquires a rule with its first writer"* enforced rather than hoped for. Watched failing twice: a stale SPEC count (red, `says 26, the zome says 27`) and a retargeted branch (red four ways, naming both types). Added to `check-spec-drift.mjs` rather than as a ninth checker, so the gate count stays **8**. | [M] | **done 2026-10-25** |

But `require_attestation_from`'s own doc comment says *"this still requires `min_attestations` distinct attesters, just from anyone"*, and `describe_method` renders *"attestation lens: N distinct attester(s) required, …, roots unrestricted"* into two published reads (`dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs` 5467, 8487). **So `{require_attestation_from: None, min_attestations: 5}` filters NOTHING and reports that it required five.** This is 7.12's own §7b.1 discipline inverted: that row insisted a read returning three of a hundred must say so, and this read returns all hundred while claiming a lens. **The fix is not obvious and that IS the finding** — enforcing the count is implementable and still worthless as a sybil defence, because the quantity it counts is free (`docs/sybil-cost-and-the-root-set.md` §2.1), so returning `true` may be the right behaviour and the DOCS may be the defect. | 1.16 | ~~**`check-ci-scope.mjs` read a YAML COMMENT as an invocation, and died instead of failing.**~~ *Numbered 1.16 because 1.15 was taken — the collision was made on 2026-10-26 night and corrected the same night.* **FIXED 2026-10-26 NIGHT, in the same pass that found it.** Rule 3 of that gate scans each workflow for `node scripts/...` to find the scripts it runs, then refuses any that read a document. The scan did not skip comment lines — so when `conductor.yml`'s header was rewritten to document the command that counts its own harness steps (`grep -cE '^ +node .*live-verify/...'`), the quoted shape was read as an invocation, resolved to the **directory** `scripts/live-verify`, and the checker died with a raw `EISDIR` stack trace. **The failure mode is the one this repository cares about more than the bug**: a gate that crashes says nothing about the thing it gates, and an unanchored count claim in a comment is exactly the drift the same header was being rewritten to prevent. Fixed by dropping comment lines before extracting invocations — a commented-out invocation does not run either, so no coverage is lost, and the per-script scan below it already applied that same rule to its own input. Found by building 6.6; the header's count claim is now a re-checkable command rather than a number, and is anchored so it does not match itself. | ~~M~~ **DONE** | nothing |
| 1.17 | **App-level timestamps have no declared unit, and this repository interprets them in TWO — seconds in one read, microseconds in another.** *Opened 2026-10-26 night, found while building 6.6 (`docs/the-joining-promise.md` is what made a `Constitution`'s expiry worth looking at).* **MEASURED, FUNCTION BY FUNCTION.** Two reads actually CONSULT a caller-supplied time field, and they disagree: `get_agent_constitution` drops an expired `Constitution` by comparing `expires_at` against `sys_time().as_seconds_and_nanos().0` — **seconds** (`dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs:5255`) — while `get_foresight_record` compares `reveal_deadline` against `sys_time().as_micros()` — **microseconds** (`:7360`), which is the right one THERE because `validate_pre_registration` compares the same field against the action's own timestamp in micros (`dna/integrity/src/lib.rs:1392`) and that comparison is DHT-enforced. So the unit is per-field, correct in each place for its own reasons, and **declared nowhere**. **AND THE SAME FIELD IS WRITTEN IN BOTH UNITS BY THE SAME ZOME**: `Claim.timestamp` gets seconds from `assert_expertise` (`:3375`) and `promote_mew_to_claim` (`:5106`), and micros from `publish_discourse_reading` (`:5561`) and `import_foreign_critique` (`:8635`). **WHAT IS AND IS NOT AT RISK.** Nothing consults `Claim.timestamp` — §5.22's refusal is explicit in the ordering code (*"timestamps may be DISPLAYED; they are never consulted here"*), and SPEC §678 already records `PreRegistration.timestamp` and `Revelation.timestamp` as never read — so today this is a DISPLAY and EXPORT inconsistency: a screen or an N4L consumer rendering both kinds of `Claim.timestamp` shows one of them in 1970 and the other in the year 56000. **THE TRAP IS THE EXPIRY FIELD, AND 7.17 WALKS TOWARD IT.** A caller who follows the most common harness idiom (`nowMicros()`, which `domain-index.mjs` and `attachment-links.mjs` both use for `published_at`) and then sets a real `expires_at` in the same unit gets a constitution that **can never expire**, because a micros value is ~10^5 times larger than the seconds `now` it is compared against. **Validation cannot catch it**: `validate_constitution` only requires `expires_at > published_at`, which is satisfied in either unit, so the inconsistency is invisible to the one layer that refuses things. If invitees are asked for a joining promise (7.17) with any expiry at all, this is the first thing that bites. **THE FIX IS THREE SMALL PARTS AND NONE FORKS**: declare the unit per field in SPEC (micros wherever validation compares against an action timestamp, seconds elsewhere), make the coordinator's own writes consistent per field, and state it in the client types — the third part is **already done**, in `agent-sdk/src/types.ts`'s `Constitution` doc comment and `promise-compliance.mjs`'s `nowSecs` note, which is what makes this a row rather than a surprise. **A fourth part is a judgement call**: changing `Claim.timestamp`'s unit in two coordinator functions makes existing entries' values mean something slightly different, and since nothing reads them the cost is zero today and rises the moment anything does. **AND A FIFTH PART EXISTED AND WAS NOT SEEN UNTIL IT BIT: 2026-10-27, THE DAY AFTER THIS ROW CLOSED, `docs/the-joining-promise.md`'s PAYLOADS — the ones an invitee copies verbatim — specified `published_at` and `created_at` in MICROS.** The code was right, the example was wrong, and the gate could not see it because it scanned code only. **`check-time-units.mjs` now also reads fenced code blocks in every root and `docs/` Markdown file** (prose excluded on purpose — the text around these fields legitimately *describes* the error, so a prose scan would flag SPEC §5.23 itself and this row), with its own vacuity floor, and **watched failing on the documentation half: both payloads reverted → 2 reds naming file, line and field.** It found a second instance on its first run: `docs/metabolic-biosignalling-currency-brief.md` documented `MutualCreditTransfer.timestamp` as *"Unix seconds"* — which matters because 3.4 leaves open whether that layer gets rebuilt from the brief, so the example is a future implementation's unit. **The lesson is narrower than "gate everything": a convention is violated first wherever it is COPIED rather than wherever it is computed.** **FIXED 2026-10-26 NIGHT — BEFORE 7.17 IS ADOPTED, which is the order the row asked for.** **(1) DECLARED: SPEC §5.23**, a table covering all 21 app-level time fields, with the reason each family is what it is rather than the other — `timestamp` family **micros** (104 of ~118 caller sites already were, and it matches Holochain's own `Timestamp`), `created_at`/`published_at`/`expires_at`/`start_time`/`end_time` **seconds** (three reads already assume it, and `mobile-ui` renders `published_at * 1000` as a date), `reveal_deadline` **micros and the only one with a DHT-side consequence**. **(2) MADE TRUE RATHER THAN ASSERTED OVER CODE THAT DISAGREED**: `assert_expertise` and `promote_mew_to_claim` now write `Claim.timestamp` in micros, joining the two functions that already did, and **30 call sites** across the harnesses, `mobile-ui` and `agent-sdk` were corrected — 0 mismatches remain of 186 minted time values. `nowSecs` was added to `mobile-ui`'s types (with the trap in its doc comment) and REMOVED from the SDK, where it became dead code once every write there was a `timestamp` field. **(3) GATED, because a convention nothing checks is the thing this list keeps finding rotted: `scripts/check-time-units.mjs`**, the **ninth** drift checker and the first that gates a convention rather than a count, a document or a rule. It reads both sides of the wire — the coordinator zome and every client — refuses to pass on an unrecognised expression rather than skipping quietly, requires SPEC §5.23 to still exist and still name every field it enforces, and fails if it finds fewer than 50 minted values, since a vacuous scan is how the original drift survived. **Watched failing on both sides**: `promote_mew_to_claim` reverted to seconds → 1 red naming file, line, unit found and unit declared; `promise-compliance`'s `published_at` switched to micros → 1 red with the consequence in the message. **(4) VERIFIED LIVE, not just compiled**: coordinator rebuilt, hApp repacked, and five harnesses run on a clean conductor each — `mew-lifecycle` and `expertise-ui` cover the two changed functions, `read-scope`, `domain-index` and `promise-compliance` cover the edited call sites. **All five green.** `cargo check` clean for wasm; nine checkers green; UI and SDK typecheck clean. **NO FORK**: the DNA hash derives from the integrity zome and nothing in it changed, and the one DHT-enforced comparison (`reveal_deadline`) was already micros and is untouched. | ~~M~~ **DONE** | nothing |
| 1.18 | ~~**`get_agent_constitution` returned the OLDER constitution when two were published in the same second.**~~ **FOUND AND FIXED 2026-10-27 by `promises-ui.mjs` §3**, which publishes twice in a row and asserted the second had superseded the first. It had not: the read ordered candidates by `published_at` **only**, and `published_at` is **seconds** (SPEC §5.23), so two publishes inside one second compare EQUAL and the winner was whatever order `get_links` happened to return. **This is 1.17's mirror image rather than a new kind of problem**: 1.17 was a unit stated nowhere, this is that unit's RESOLUTION being too coarse for the one read that depends on ordering. **THE FIX USES THE ONLY CLOCK §5.22 ALLOWS ANYTHING TO CONSULT**: ties break on the host-stamped **action** timestamp, which is micros and which no author can set — author-supplied `published_at` still leads, because it is what the author claims and a reader can see it, and the action timestamp only separates claims that are otherwise identical. **Coordinator-only, so no fork**: the `Record` was already in hand at that line, the entry type is untouched, and the DNA hash derives from the integrity zome. **Why it mattered beyond the harness**: 6.7's new surface tells a user that publishing again is what readers see, and that sentence was false for anyone who corrected a typo immediately — which is exactly when somebody republishes. | ~~M~~ **DONE** | — |
| 1.19 | ~~**The agent SDK is missing the protocol's central mechanism, and six other functions that already exist in the zome.**~~ **BUILT 2026-10-28 in both clients, verified against a live conductor and injected five times.** *Opened 2026-10-27 while scoping AI integration, and found by listing the SDK's surface against the coordinator's rather than by reading either one.* **MEASURED:** `grep -rn 'assess\|publish_discourse\|past_cone\|pre_register' agent-sdk/src` returns **nothing**. The coordinator has `publish_assessment`, `get_assessments_for`, `publish_discourse_reading`, `publish_settlement_policy`, `publish_settlement_status`, `recompute_settlement`, `pre_register` and `reveal_pre_registration`; `agent-sdk/src/index.ts` exposes **none** of them. **THE WORST OF IT IS FORESIGHT.** `pre_register` and `reveal_pre_registration` are the denominator — the mechanism every outward-facing document leads with — and an SDK agent cannot reach them at all. So an AI agent can publish claims, critique, retract and join a membrane, but **cannot make or reveal a pre-registered commitment**, which is the one thing this protocol exists to do. **AND IT MIRRORS §6 EXACTLY, which is the tell that this is one defect rather than eight:** the SDK has `discourseHealth()` (`index.ts:547`) and not `publishDiscourseReading` — *the read is wired, the publish is not*, the same sentence §6's preamble uses about `mobile-ui`. The gap is not random: **reads got surfaced and writes did not, in both clients, independently.** **WHY THIS IS `[M]` AND NOT `[D]`**, unlike its §6 siblings: §6's rows are blocked on how to render a thing honestly to a *human* — 6.2 needs a lens picker, 6.4 a screen, 6.5 asks what a client may assert on its user's behalf. **An SDK agent needs no screen and speaks under its own key**, so none of those decisions gates this. The zome functions exist, their payload types are specified, and `SPEC.md` is the authority. **DO NOT LET IT BECOME AN ABSTRACTION** — `agent-sdk/README.md` is explicit that the library *"is not an abstraction over the protocol and should not become one"*, and each addition should be typed payloads plus the protocol's norms, nothing more. **`mcp-server/` must follow in the same pass**, since it is a thin wrapper where every tool maps to one SDK call; a function added to the SDK and not to MCP is discoverable by nobody. **BUILT, IN BOTH, AND THE MCP HALF WAS NOT OPTIONAL** — stopping at the SDK would have been this row's own defect one layer up. `agent-sdk` gained all eight plus `witnessedCritiquesOf`, `MAX_PAST_CONE` and `randomSalt`; `mcp-server` gained **ten tools, 9 to 19**, and `agent-sdk.mjs` went **34 checks to 67**, `mcp-server.mjs` **21 to 52**, all green on a live conductor. **THE PAST CONE WAS THE ONLY DECISION, and it is 6.5 narrowed rather than 6.5 answered:** 6.5 refuses to let a *client* populate `past_cone` because it would assert what its *user* read, and an over-claiming client makes its user look better informed than they were. For an agent under its own key that victim does not exist — the agent **is** the reader — so `witnessedCritiquesOf` reads the critiques and returns the cone of exactly the hashes it fetched, which makes the convenient path the accurate one without making the claim unfalsifiable. MCP's `assess` does the same read at call time rather than taking the caller's word, with an explicit `record_what_i_read: false` to opt out, because *recorded nothing* and *saw nothing* are different statements and the protocol keeps them apart. **`preRegister` RETURNS THE SALT, ALWAYS, generated when not supplied** — the zome cannot invent one without storing it in the entry it protects, but a client can generate one and hand it back without storing it anywhere, and a conversational agent is the caller most likely to drop it; the MCP response says KEEP THE SALT in the payload rather than only in the schema. **FOUR DEFECTS FOUND BY BUILDING IT, three of them pre-existing:** (1) `mcp-server`'s `discourse_health` passed a domain NAME into `discourseHealth`, which takes a membrane hash — the handler's args are `Record<string, any>` so TypeScript could not object, and **the tool had never worked for any caller in any published release**; it survived because `mcp-server.mjs` never called it, and the two tools it never called were the two taking a domain. Confirmed broken on a live conductor before the fix, which resolves the name through `membraneFor` and names the existing domains when it cannot. (2) `SettlementPolicy.published_at` minted in micros where §5.23 declares seconds — caught by `check-time-units.mjs`, invisible at every other layer since it type-checks, validates and round-trips, and only an **expiry** in the wrong unit misbehaves. The comment on `nowMicros` had predicted exactly this and said a seconds helper "would currently be dead code". (3) `get_my_sealed_prediction`'s *"Target is not a PreRegistration."* was unreachable for the input it was written for: a Claim's hash resolves and simply is not that type, so deserialization failed first and the caller saw a raw serde error. (4) `agent-sdk.mjs`'s decisive reveal was unguarded, so a lossy-read injection turned validation's correct refusal into an uncaught throw that killed the run before four later sections — the same fragility `foresight-ui.mjs`'s header records, found the same way. **FOUNDING IS BUILT IN BOTH CLIENTS AND VERIFIED — 2026-10-28, five injections, and the counts are updated because the harnesses now cover it.** `agent-sdk` gained `publishConstitution`, `createMembrane` and `ownConstitution`; `mcp-server` gained `publish_my_constitution` and `found_domain`, **21 tools from 19**. `agent-sdk.mjs` went **67 checks to 93**, `mcp-server.mjs` **52 to 73**, all green on a live conductor, and the figures in `README.md` and `scripts/live-verify/README.md` are bumped from nineteen *now that nineteen is no longer what is verified*. **WHY THIS WAS A GAP AT ALL, beyond the eight:** an SDK agent could JOIN a membrane and could not FOUND one, so it could only ever participate in domains a human had opened — and `create_membrane` needs a constitution's ActionHash while `get_agent_constitution` returns the ENTRY, so founding was unreachable even for an agent that HAD published one. That is what `ownConstitution` exists for. **THE DESIGN DECISION, so it is not re-litigated:** `createMembrane` takes the ActionHash of an ALREADY-PUBLISHED constitution and will not mint one. Founding here is accountable rather than costly (README §2.6) — no fee, but the founder must have stated their own promises in public first — and a call that published the constitution for you would leave that requirement technically satisfied and substantively empty, since nobody would ever have chosen what they were promising. *Resolving* the hash of an existing one is NOT that convenience: an agent with no constitution gets a refusal naming §2.6 and founds nothing. **`ownConstitution` DUPLICATES THE COORDINATOR'S SELECTION RULE** (expired dropped, newest `published_at` in SECONDS, ties broken by the host action timestamp), which is a defect generator, so it compares its pick against `get_agent_constitution` and THROWS on disagreement rather than founding a membrane against one constitution while every reader sees another. **THE HARNESS SECTIONS' ORDER IS A TEST AND NOT A STYLE:** the refusals come BEFORE the first publish, because *no constitution yet → founds nothing* is the central assertion and is reachable only while the agent still has none — a published constitution is permanent and nothing later in a run can restore that state. And the two constitutions are published MORE THAN A SECOND APART on purpose: `published_at` is seconds, so a same-second tie falls through to the host action timestamp and the ordering becomes untestable. **THE RAW `call` ESCAPE HATCH IS GONE FROM BOTH HARNESSES** — the discourse reading and the domain-name tools used to be seeded through it with hand-written snake_case payloads, and are now seeded by the typed calls and the tools under test, so the hatch's disappearance IS the verification: founding breaking now takes four and seven later checks red with it rather than none. **THREE DEFECTS FOUND BY BUILDING IT, and one of them by writing the harness rather than by running it:** (1) `create_membrane`'s *"Referenced entry is not a Constitution."* was UNREACHABLE for a Claim's ActionHash — `to_app_option` failed before the arm carrying it and the founder got `Guest("Deserialize(\"missing field `agent`\")")` — **the same defect shape as `get_my_sealed_prediction` hours earlier, found the same way, in a second function**; now on both arms. (2) **`found_domain`'s handler had always read `a.constitution_hash` and its SCHEMA NEVER DECLARED IT.** The tool list is the whole of what an agent knows about a tool, so the parameter was unreachable by any agent — while `publish_my_constitution`'s own note told the caller to KEEP THIS HASH because *passing it explicitly is the only way to be certain which constitution the domain references*: advice that could not be followed through the surface that gave it. The harness could send the undeclared argument, which is exactly why the handler checks could not catch it and discoverability needed its own check against the schema. (3) **the century ceiling `publishConstitution` was documented as having did not exist** — the `expires_at > published_at` rule is TRUE IN EITHER UNIT, so a micros expiry passes the coordinator, passes validation and round-trips intact, and the constitution never expires while its author believes it does; the comment described the silent case while accepting it. **AND A WRONG-HASH GUARD ADDED, with the prefixes MEASURED rather than remembered** (ActionHash `132,41,36`, EntryHash `132,33,36`, AgentPubKey `132,32,36`, all 39 bytes): handing a typed struct an EntryHash where it declares an ActionHash fails while deserialising the WHOLE PAYLOAD, before the function body, and the caller sees `Deserialize([135, 166, ...])` — the msgpack bytes of their own arguments as a decimal array, naming nothing. It is also the mistake most available to a model, since both clients hand back `entry_hash` and `action_hash` side by side and critique targets ARE entry hashes. **THE FIVE INJECTIONS, because checks written against code written the same day are the ones most likely to assert nothing.
