| 1.23 | **`render()` rebuilds the whole DOM, and the architecture was never a decision — it was a default nobody had argued for.** *Opened and DECIDED 2026-11-02, after two bugs had already been paid for.* **MEASURED:** `render()` is `app.innerHTML = ''` plus a full rebuild (`main.ts:669`), reached from **29 call sites**, fired by **23 fire-and-forget `void load*()` calls** across **20 async loaders**. #418 and #419 fixed two symptoms of it — a harness flake and real typing loss — by ADDING capture machinery rather than by changing anything. **THE FACT THAT REFRAMES IT, and it was sitting in the file the whole time: there are already TWELVE module-level `Set`/`Map` state holders whose only job is to survive a rebuild** (`expandedClaims`, `graphOpenClaims`, `chainOpen`, `resonanceOpen`, `unseenOpen`, `lensBuilderOpen`, `openPeriods`, and the rest). So the architecture is **already** *UI state lives outside the DOM; the DOM is a pure function of it* — the same discipline a declarative framework enforces, hand-rolled and nowhere written down. **The rebuild is not wrong. It is correct given an invariant nobody had stated**, and every bug it has produced was a case where the invariant did not hold because the state belonged to the BROWSER rather than to the app. **DECIDED: KEEP IT, NAME THE INVARIANT, GATE IT.** The alternative considered and rejected was per-region rendering — each loader re-rendering only its own subtree. That removes this bug class and **trades it for a worse one**: a global rebuild that drops browser state fails LOUDLY and in a BOUNDED list (caret, selection, field value, scroll, media playback — three of five already handled), while a stale subtree or a missed invalidation fails SILENTLY and in an unbounded one. It would also rewrite 29 sites and 20 loaders for a defect nobody is currently reporting. **A framework was not seriously considered**: this file's own preamble records minimal dependencies over convenience as a settled preference, and adopting one now would be re-deciding that in passing. **SO THE WORK IS THREE THINGS, none of them a rewrite:** write the invariant into `main.ts` where the next person will read it before adding state; add a gate that fails when component-local state is held in the DOM instead of lifted; and **MEASURE what a rebuild actually destroys before extending the capture helpers** — page scroll is the obvious candidate and is UNVERIFIED, because `innerHTML = ''` and the appends happen in one synchronous block and the browser may never reflow in between. **Fixing a bug I have not watched happen is the thing this repository keeps recording as the expensive mistake**, so the measurement comes first and the harness is what decides whether `captureScroll` exists. **BUILT 2026-11-02, AND THE MEASUREMENT CHANGED WHAT GOT BUILT.** `scripts/live-verify/render-invariant.mjs`, 21 checks, in `ui.yml`. **PAGE SCROLL NEEDS NO FIX AND `captureScroll` DOES NOT EXIST:** scroll survives a rebuild mid-page AND at the very bottom, because `innerHTML = ''` and the appends are one synchronous block and the browser never reflows in between — measured at maximum scroll with the content growing, so not a clamp either. **THE ONE REAL GAP WAS SOMETHING ELSE ENTIRELY: focus on a BUTTON or link did not survive**, falling to `BODY`, so a keyboard user's next Tab restarted from the top of the document. **AND `captureFocus`'s OWN COMMENT ASSERTED THE OPPOSITE** — *"a render caused by pressing a button leaves the button focused"* — which is precisely what justified the function handling text boxes ONLY. The claim was false and the scope it justified was wrong with it: 1.20's bug class in the docstring explaining why the code is shaped as it is. Fixed with `controlKey`/`controlsWithKey` and a `preventScroll` restore, where **`preventScroll` is load-bearing** because a bare `.focus()` scrolls its target into view and would have introduced the jump §1 had just proved does not happen. **AND THE FIRST MEASUREMENT WAS AN ARTIFACT THAT AGREED WITH MY HYPOTHESIS, which is the dangerous kind.** `locator.click()` scrolls its target into view BEFORE clicking, so a run that scrolled to the bottom and clicked a toggle near the TOP reported scroll as MOVED by four thousand pixels — Playwright's scroll read as the app's — while the mid-page case, where the target happened to be visible, reported PRESERVED and matched what I expected. Both numbers were about the instrument. Every trigger is now a DOM-level `el.click()` inside `page.evaluate`, and §2 asserts the clicked element was OFF SCREEN and the page still did not move, which is the control that makes any of it about `render()`. **THE GATE IS BEHAVIOURAL RATHER THAN STATIC, which is the other thing the build settled.** There is no mechanical way to check *no component holds state in the DOM*; what there is, is a harness that fails when a rebuild drops something — so the invariant is enforced by consequence. Injections **CX** (`captureFieldValues` no-op) 1 red with the focus half green, **CY** (control restore no-op) 2 red, **CZ** (restores moved out of `finally`) **GREEN — a recorded LIMIT**: the early returns `finally` exists for are the connect and notes-without-conductor screens, which this harness never drives. **Next free injection ID: DA.** | ~~**M**~~ **DONE 2026-11-02** | nothing — decided, built, gated |
| 1.24 | **The commitment index is a convention, not a rule — a client can commit and stay out of its own denominator.** *Opened 2026-11-02, found by fact-checking 7.24's outline rather than by any gate.* **MEASURED:** the denominator is a `get_links` over `AgentToPreRegistration` (`coordinator:7619`); the link is created by the coordinator's `pre_register` (`coordinator:7456`); and **`validate_pre_registration` contains ZERO references to that link type.** What validation enforces is that such a link, IF PRESENT, targets a real `PreRegistration` — it never requires one to exist. **`attempt_raw_pre_registration` (`coordinator:2392`) writes the entry with no link at all**, and that prober already ships. So an author running a modified client can hold a sealed commitment that no reader's count includes. **THIS IS THE THIRD INSTANCE OF ONE SHAPE AND THAT IS THE REAL FINDING:** `required_evidence` declared and unread (7.25, still one declaration and zero readers, re-verified 2026-11-02), `min_attestations` claimed as enforced by a read that published the lie (1.12), and now an index described outward as validation-enforced when it is coordinator convention. **Each was caught by somebody writing an outward-facing sentence, never by a gate** — which is 1.20's argument arriving a third time, and an argument for a gate that pairs each *enforced* claim with the validator that enforces it. **WHY IT IS NOT MERELY A PROSE FIX:** the denominator is the one property this protocol's outward-facing material leads with. A reader who can skip the index can also decline to be counted, which is precisely the invisible-denominator problem the mechanism exists to remove. **THE FIX IS PROBABLY A READ AND THEREFORE CHEAP, AND IT IS STILL A `[D]`.** Counting from the author's chain via `must_get_agent_activity` — already proven in this codebase, it is how the friction check scans (`integrity:2316`) — cannot be skipped, because writing the entry puts it on the chain whatever the client does. That would be **coordinator-only, so no DNA hash change and no fork**. The decision it carries is what the denominator IS: an index a writer opts into, or an enumeration of a chain that an observer must be able to fetch. The second is stronger and changes the answer for any observer whose arc cannot reach that chain, which is not nothing on a partition-tolerant DHT. **DO NOT PUBLISH THE STRONG CLAIM UNTIL THIS IS BUILT** — 7.24's outline now carries the honest version in §3b and the gap in §5. **DECIDED 2026-11-02: THE DENOMINATOR IS THE CHAIN ENUMERATION. The index stays as a fast path, and the report names which source answered.** **WHAT KILLED THE OBJECTION, and it was the only real one.** The case for keeping the index was availability: a chain read depends on reaching the agent-activity neighbourhood, and a link read supposedly does not. **It does.** `AgentToPreRegistration` hangs off `agent_anchor_hash(&agent)`, so counting it is a `get_links` against that anchor's neighbourhood — **both routes are DHT neighbourhood reads that can fail**, and the index's only distinguishing property turns out to be that *a writer can decline to appear in it*. There is no availability advantage to buy the hole with. **THE MECHANISM, CHECKED AGAINST THE HDK RATHER THAN ASSUMED** (`hdk-0.7.0/src/chain.rs:29`). `get_agent_activity(agent, query, ActivityRequest::Full)` returns **action sequences and hashes** for actions matching a `ChainQueryFilter`, filtered **at the source** before transmission, and the activity is held by the neighbourhood of the agent's public key. Hashes are enough — a denominator needs a COUNT, and the commitments are sealed anyway, so nothing here reads content it should not. `must_get_agent_activity` is already trusted in the integrity zome for the friction scan (`integrity:2316`), so this is a proven dependency rather than a new one. **AND THE REPLY CARRIES ITS OWN STATUS, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THE HONEST VERSION POSSIBLE.** `get_agent_activity` reports whether the chain is valid, contains an invalid item, is **forked**, or is empty, plus any warrants. So *I could not see this chain* is distinguishable from *there are none* — the distinction the rest of this protocol is built on (`cone_recorded` gating `unseen`, `total_known` as one observer's count, `horizon_exhausted` on `get_standing`). **A count that could not reach the chain must report that and not a smaller number**, because a silently-smaller denominator is the exact defect being fixed. A forked chain is information about the author rather than an error. **WHY NOT DROP THE INDEX.** It is a cheap read for the author's own screen and it is already written and validated; removing it is a change with no benefit. It becomes a **hint**, never the answer, and the report says which one it used — the same division `renderUnseenState` keeps between the client's figures and the protocol's note. **COST ACCEPTED:** a chain read per agent is dearer than a link read, and the figure now depends on a neighbourhood the observer may not reach — which was always true of the index too, and is now *reported* instead of silently absorbed. **NOT DECIDED HERE:** whether validation should additionally REFUSE a `PreRegistration` whose link is missing. It should not be needed once the count no longer depends on the link, and it would mean an integrity change and a fork for a property the read already has. **BUILT 2026-11-02 — AND THE MEASUREMENT SAYS THE CHAIN ROUTE DOES NOT WORK ON THIS SUBSTRATE, WHICH IS THE FINDING.** `get_agent_activity` returns `ChainStatus::Empty` with no activity for an agent whose index demonstrably holds commitments — **for their own chain AND for another agent's**, at 0, 5, 10, 20 and 30 seconds, with `GetOptions::default()` already being `network()` (checked, not assumed). Two agents, one conductor, holochain 0.7.0. So the decision above is right and **currently unavailable**, which is a fact about the substrate rather than about the design. **WHAT SHIPPED IS THE HONEST DEGRADATION, AND IT IS A REAL IMPROVEMENT ON ITS OWN.** `get_foresight_record` now carries `counted_from`, `chain_status`, `unindexed` and `note`. When the chain cannot be read it falls back to the index **and says the number is a FLOOR rather than a denominator** — where before it silently presented the index AS the denominator. The UI renders that caveat from the coordinator's own `note` whenever `counted_from != "chain"`, which on this substrate is always. SPEC's §10 row now states both sources and their different standing. **MY FIRST IMPLEMENTATION REINTRODUCED THE DEFECT IT WAS FIXING, AND THE PROBE CAUGHT IT.** It treated `ChainStatus::Empty` as a successful count of ZERO — a silently smaller denominator, inside the fix for silently smaller denominators. An empty chain beside a non-empty index is **unreadable, not zero**, and the two can only disagree in that direction because the chain is a superset of the index by construction. **Reasoning did not catch this; running it did.** **AND AN EXISTING GUARD EARNED ITS KEEP:** `pre-registration.mjs` asserted `ForesightRecord` has EXACTLY three keys, and went red the moment provenance was added. That is what an exact key set is for — a new field on this read is a decision, because what it guards is that no SCORE ever appears. Updated to the new exact set rather than loosened, with a control asserting the one number it carries is about the INDEX's completeness and not about an agent. `pre-registration.mjs` 27 to 36 checks. ~~**WHAT IS LEFT, AND IT IS NOT A `[D]`:** find out whether `get_agent_activity` serves a chain on a real multi-conductor network~~ **MEASURED 2026-11-08, AND THE ARM IS NOT DEAD CODE.** On a settled multi-member DHT (`network.sh` A/B plus `start-node nodeD`) `get_agent_activity` returns `ChainStatus::Valid` and `counted_from` is `"chain"` — read by the author, and by **two peer observers for whom the activity authority cannot be self**, at 0, 15 and 30 seconds. So the `ChainStatus::Empty` recorded above was a property of the one-conductor sandbox and not of the code, and the decision taken here was available all along. **7.24's §3b no longer needs the unavailability caveat — it needs a different one**, because the same measurement found the chain route under-counts briefly after a write: **that is row 1.25**, and §3b must carry its honest version instead. | ~~**D**~~ **DECIDED AND BUILT 2026-11-02; the chain route MEASURED WORKING 2026-11-08 — see 1.25** | nothing — coordinator-only, no DNA change, no fork (hash verified identical) |
| 1.25 | **The chain-derived count under-counts for ~5 seconds after a write, and `unindexed` is structurally unable to say so.** *Opened 2026-11-08, found by the multi-node measurement 1.24 asked for — run on the author's word AFTER #436 merged, which is the wrong order and is why this row exists.* **WHAT WAS MEASURED.** Writing a commitment and polling the author's OWN record on nodeA, three members on the shared DHT: at **+0s** `counted_from=chain chain_status=valid pending=6 unindexed=0` and the commitment just written is **absent**; at **+5s** `pending=7` and present. `get_pre_registrations_for_question` returned it **throughout**, including while the record denied it. Transient and converging, not structural. **IT FALSIFIES A LOAD-BEARING COMMENT IN THE CODE THAT SHIPPED** (`coordinator:7705`): *"the chain is a superset of the index by construction, because the index is written from the same call that writes the entry."* That holds for a LOCAL index against a LOCAL chain. It is false the moment the chain is served by a **remote activity authority**, whose view lags the write — and the remote authority is the only reason `counted_from: "chain"` is reachable at all, so the branch that makes the fix available is the branch that breaks the invariant the fix was reasoned from. **AND THE FIELD THAT EXISTS TO REPORT DISAGREEMENT CANNOT REPORT THIS ONE:** `unindexed` counts, by construction, only the chain hashes MISSING FROM THE INDEX — and never the index hashes missing from the chain, which is the direction the lag actually runs. It read `0` at every sample, including the samples where the index held one more commitment than the chain did. A reader is told the two sources agree, at the one moment they do not. **WHY IT IS NOT COSMETIC, AND THIS IS THE WHOLE ARGUMENT:** the moment a denominator is read is the moment somebody has just committed — the screen is open BECAUSE they acted. So the ~5-second window is not a rare race, it is the common path, and what it produces is a **silently smaller denominator**: the exact defect 1.24 exists to remove, reintroduced by 1.24's own fix, pointing the other way. 1.24's first implementation did this too (empty chain read as zero) and the probe caught it; this one the probe could not catch, for the reason below. **THE GATE IS GREEN BECAUSE ITS SUBSTRATE NEVER TAKES THE BRANCH.** `pre-registration.mjs` against nodeA with the chain arm live: **7 checks FAIL** — `sits in PENDING`, `the sealed prediction is NOT readable`, `moves to REVEALED`, and the question-binding pair. On one conductor the index arm is taken and every check passes, and `conductor` and `network` install extra agents on a SINGLE conductor rather than forming a multi-member DHT. **Main was green on all five gates at `e13084b` while carrying this** — a real green, silent on the arm that shipped. **That is the fourth instance of the shape 1.24 named as its own real finding** (after `required_evidence` unread, `min_attestations` claimed-and-unenforced, and the index described as validation-enforced), and the first where the unexercised code is code THIS row's predecessor added. **TWO THINGS THE MEASUREMENT DID NOT ESTABLISH, STATED SO THEY ARE NOT READ AS SETTLED.** (a) The first run, on a freshly started TWO-member network, still said `counted_from=index chain_status=unreadable`; between it and the positive result **nodeD was started AND time passed**, so *needs a third member* and *needs time to settle* were never tested against each other. (b) Of the 7 failures, the membership ones follow from the 5-second lag, but **`CONTROL: the one number it does carry is about the INDEX, not the agent` looks like an assertion written FOR the index arm** rather than a product defect — those two causes were not separated, and the fix for each is different. **THE DECISION.** Whether the read should **prefer the local index when it is a strict superset of what the authority served** — which is sound in exactly the direction the lag runs, costs one set comparison, stays coordinator-only, and would make `unindexed` honest by giving it a counterpart; or whether a count that disagrees with the chain must report the disagreement and let the reader hold a floor. **WHAT MUST NOT HAPPEN, because it was already tried once on row 7.28:** giving `pre-registration.mjs` a settle-wait so the chain arm goes green is **printing a clean PASS on a weaker assertion** unless the under-count is fixed first — the probe would then assert that the count is right five seconds after a write, which is not the property anyone needs. The wait is worth adding so the arm can be asserted AT ALL; it is not a fix, and ordering matters. **DECIDED AND BUILT 2026-11-08: PREFER THE LOCAL INDEX WHEN IT IS A STRICT SUPERSET.** Both directions of disagreement are now computed — `missing` is chain-not-in-index, which is 1.24's finding, and `unserved` is index-not-in-chain, which is the authority behind the write. When `missing == 0 && unserved > 0` the count is the index and `counted_from` is a THIRD value, **`"index-superset"`**, which is deliberately not a weaker `"index"`: the chain WAS read, nothing on it was unindexed, and the index merely held more. Only `"index"` is the floor, and `"index-superset"` must never be rendered as one — a reader told to treat it as a floor would discount the commitment they had just made, which inverts the screen's job. **THE MIXED CASE IS REPORTED AND NOT COUNTED, deliberately:** when the two disagree in BOTH directions the count stays the chain, because that is the source a writer cannot skip, and the note states how many it is short by. **Counting the union would be a different decision than the one taken and was not taken here.** **MEASURED ON BOTH SUBSTRATES, because the whole point of this row is that one substrate cannot see the defect.** Three-member DHT (`network.sh` plus `start-node nodeD`): `counted_from=index-superset chain_status=valid unindexed=0`, **37 checks green, the 7 failures gone**. Single conductor: `counted_from=index chain_status=unreadable`, **36 checks green, the index arm untouched**. `hc dna hash` returns `uhC0kb-i4w7LvWd-pzqG3Wmm76P0U4pFZ3yGXhWzp-aRCpinsVDdh`, byte-identical to shipped v0.3.1, so coordinator-only holds and no fork. **AND THE ONE FAILURE THIS ROW COULD NOT ATTRIBUTE WAS THE PROBE'S, WHICH IS NOW SEPARATED.** §7's `CONTROL: the one number it does carry is about the INDEX` required the word *index* in the note. Every substrate it had ever run on took either the index arm or a chain arm with `unindexed > 0`, so the word was always present and the requirement looked free; on a settled DHT the chain is read, both sources agree, and there is nothing true to say about the index. **The check was asserting where the harness was running rather than what the field means** — it now asserts that the note names its SOURCE and leaves which source to the substrate. **STILL UNRESOLVED, and it is the same confound as before:** whether the chain becomes readable because of a THIRD member or merely because time passed was never tested, since nodeD was started and time passed together. | ~~**D**~~ **DECIDED AND BUILT 2026-11-08** | nothing — coordinator-only read, no DNA change, no fork (hash verified identical) |
| 1.26 | **A claim cannot cite another claim, so the protocol's own advice produces claims whose dependency it cannot express.** *Opened 2026-11-08, found by the FIRST PILOT PARTICIPANT on their first claim — and found because of advice given here, which is the part worth keeping.* **WHAT HAPPENED.** They brought a bundled claim: an empirical half (in a system with waiting lists, every place has a cost — someone else is waiting) and a normative half (care should come with criteria and end when no attempt is made). The advice was to SPLIT it, and the reason was structural: a `Critique` targets a WHOLE claim and carries exactly ONE `CritiqueMode`, so a critic who accepts the cost argument and rejects the discharge rule has nowhere to put that — the most interesting position in the room is unsayable, and a critique that lands leaves no record of which half fell. **They split it. The split is right. And now nothing can say that the second claim rests on the first.** `Claim` carries `content`, `domain`, `author`, `timestamp`, `evidence_hashes`, `confidence`, `semantic_tags` and `source_mew` — there is no claim-to-claim relation of any kind. **SO THE GAP IS GENERATED BY THE FIX, WHICH IS WHY IT IS A ROW AND NOT A NICETY:** splitting is the correct response to one-mode-per-critique, and splitting is exactly what creates a dependency the graph cannot hold. A critic who refutes claim 1 does not visibly touch claim 2, though claim 2 is worthless without it, and the only thing joining them is a shared `domain` and the wording. **THREE PATHS, AND THEIR COSTS ARE NOT CLOSE.** **(a) `evidence_hashes`, AND IT ALREADY VALIDATES TODAY.** `validate_claim` requires each hash to resolve to SOME entry and DELIBERATELY NOT to `Evidence` (`integrity:1337`, and the note beside it says so in as many words), so claim 1's EntryHash in claim 2's `evidence_hashes` is accepted right now with no change anywhere. **But nothing reads it as support**, and the field's name says *evidence* to every reader — SPEC, the SDK and the UI all present it that way. Overloading it silently would be meaning with no reader, which is the half-surfaced shape this list keeps recording, and 7.25 already has `required_evidence` declared-and-unread on the same field family. **(b) A claim-to-claim `SynapticLink` — coordinator-only, no DNA change, no fork.** `create_synaptic_link` (`coordinator:792`) takes `impl Into<AnyLinkableHash>` for base and target, so claim-to-claim is expressible with the types that already ship. **AND IT HAS A TRAP THAT MAKES THIS A `[D]` RATHER THAN AN `[M]`:** `direct_attesters` treats a `SynapticLink` pointing at an agent's Claim as a DIRECT ATTESTATION of that agent and collects `link.author` **with no self-exclusion** (`coordinator:4508` and `:4520`, verified). So an author citing their own claim would enter their OWN attester list, minting self-attestation out of a citation. Whether that is reachable as an exploit depends on root-set membership and was NOT traced here — **TRACED 2026-11-08 and it is REACHABLE: see row 1.27.** A candidate who is themselves in the reader's root set counts as one of their own attesters, so this option would add a second free route to a hole that is already live via self-critique — but a citation that contaminates the trust lens is not a cosmetic concern. Taking this path means either adding a self-exclusion to `direct_attesters`, which is cheap but CHANGES AN EXISTING LENS'S ANSWERS and so is its own decision, or accepting the contamination, which is not acceptable. The conductance-and-atrophy semantics are a second question on this path: a support relation that decays like a critique connection may or may not be wanted, and nobody has said. **(c) A distinct typed link.** Clean semantics, and an INTEGRITY change — new `LinkTypes` variant, new DNA hash, a fork. That puts it behind §4's one-fork rule and it should accumulate there rather than travel alone. **WHAT THE DECISION ACTUALLY IS**, stated so it is not mistaken for a plumbing choice: whether *rests on* is a relation this protocol holds at all. Today the graph has one edge between epistemic objects — a critique against its target, which is DISAGREEMENT. Adding support means the graph carries agreement too, and every read that walks critique edges (standing, discourse health, the unseen cone, attestation) would have a second edge type in scope and would have to say whether it follows it. **THAT IS THE WHOLE COST, and it is larger than any of the three implementations.** **NOT BLOCKED ON THE PILOT, AND THE PILOT SHOULD NOT WAIT FOR IT:** the participant was told plainly that the dependency will not show on screen, that it is our gap and not their wording, and nothing about publishing two claims in one domain is broken. **AND THE ADVICE STANDS REGARDLESS** — split, don't bundle. A dependency the graph cannot express is a smaller defect than a critique that cannot say which half it refuted. | **D** | (a) nothing; (b) nothing — coordinator-only; (c) §4's integrity fork |
| 1.27 | **An agent in the reader's own root set counts as one of their own attesters, so `min_attestations` overstates independent vouching by exactly one.** *Opened 2026-11-08 while tracing the question 1.26 recorded as NOT TRACED — and the answer is that 1.26's cheap option would not CREATE this, it would widen it.* **THE TRACE, read rather than reasoned.** `count_attestations_pure` (`coordinator:4539`) inserts the candidate into `visited` and then, for each attester, checks `roots.contains(attester)` **before any self-exclusion, of which there is none**. `direct_attesters_of` (`coordinator:4496`) collects `link.author` for every `SynapticLink` pointing at the candidate's claims and never compares that author to the candidate. **So the two cases diverge:** a candidate NOT in the root set is protected, because the `else if max_depth > 0` branch recurses and the cycle guard returns 0 — which is precisely what that guard's comment claims it does. A candidate who **IS** in the root set is not protected at all: their own link satisfies `roots.contains` directly, and counts. **CONSEQUENCE, stated narrowly because the narrow version is the true one:** for a reader with `require_attestation_from: [A, B]` and `min_attestations: 2`, candidate A needs two root-set attesters and can supply one of them themselves — **one genuine voucher plus one self-link clears a two-voucher bar.** It cannot manufacture trust from nothing: an agent outside the root set gains exactly zero, so this never promotes a stranger. What it does is **overstate independent vouching by one for every agent the reader has already named**, which is the population where `min_attestations` is the only thing still doing work. **THE SELF-LINK IS FREE AND ALREADY REACHABLE — THIS IS LIVE IN SHIPPED CODE, NOT A CONSEQUENCE OF 1.26.** `create_critique` writes a `SynapticLink` to the target claim's hash, and `validate_critique`'s only author rule is that `critique.author` matches the action author — anti-spoofing, never anti-self (`integrity:1691`). So an agent may critique their own claim, and that is all it takes. 1.26's option (b) — a claim-to-claim `SynapticLink` for citation — would add a SECOND free route to the same place, which is why that option's cost is now known rather than suspected. **IT IS A REFINEMENT OF 3.7 RATHER THAN A NEW SHAPE.** 3.7 established that `direct_attesters_of` unions a tenure-gated source with a free global one and that *a union costs what its cheapest member costs*; `docs/sybil-cost-and-the-root-set.md` carries the argument. **This row names the cheapest member yet found: the candidate.** Minting K keys costs K critiques; self-attesting costs one, from a key the reader has already chosen to trust. **NO UNIT TEST COVERS IT, AND THE NEAREST ONE LOOKS LIKE IT DOES.** Seven tests exercise the walk — direct, non-root at depth zero, transitive allowed, transitive blocked, **a two-agent A-attests-B-attests-A cycle that must terminate**, multiple distinct attesters, and the empty graph (`coordinator:6799-6889`). The two-cycle test is the one a reader would assume covers this and does not: **A to B to A is not A to A**, and only the second reaches the `roots.contains` branch before the guard. **THE DECISION, AND IT IS WHY THIS IS A `[D]`.** Excluding self is two lines in either function, but it **CHANGES AN EXISTING LENS'S ANSWERS** — every historical `is_agent_attested` verdict involving a root-set self-link flips, and under Invariant 6 nothing already written changes, so readers would get a different answer tomorrow about the same permanent records. That is a real decision rather than a fix to apply quietly. The alternative is to leave it and DOCUMENT that `min_attestations` counts self for named roots, which is honest and makes the threshold mean something different from what a reader would assume. **DO NOT ADD A UNIT TEST FIRST:** a test written against today's behaviour would enshrine the hole as intended, which is the one outcome worse than the hole. The test follows the decision. **DECIDED AND BUILT 2026-11-08: SELF IS EXCLUDED.** Excluded at BOTH layers, because they fail for different reasons — in `direct_attesters_of` the returned list would otherwise be wrong for every consumer, and in `count_attestations_pure` the guarantee belongs to the walk rather than to one of its callers, since that function takes an arbitrary closure. §2.6 is defended at two layers for the same kind of reason. **In the pure walk the check must come BEFORE the roots check**, which is the whole defect restated as an ordering constraint. The `AttestationGrant` source is excluded too: the integrity zome has no reason to refuse a self-grant, since its checks are tenure, budget and authorship and a self-grant violates none of them. **THE TESTS WERE WATCHED FAILING BEFORE THEY WERE BELIEVED, and the injection proved three things at once.** With the pure-walk exclusion removed: both new tests go RED (`...self_attestation_by_a_root_member_does_not_count`, `...self_link_cannot_pad_a_two_attester_threshold`), the new CONTROL stays green — correctly, since a genuine attester counts with or without the fix, and an assertion that only ever goes down is indistinguishable from a function returning zero — and **all seven original tests stay green, which demonstrates rather than asserts that they never covered this.** 80 tests pass restored; the attestation group is 10, from 7. **DNA HASH VERIFIED UNCHANGED** — `uhC0kb-i4w7LvWd-pzqG3Wmm76P0U4pFZ3yGXhWzp-aRCpinsVDdh`, byte-identical, so coordinator-only holds and nothing forks. **TWO PROSE CLAIMS WERE INVALIDATED BY THE FIX AND ARE CORRECTED IN THE SAME PASS, which is 1.20's bug class caught on the way past:** `README` §197 said *"Seven unit tests cover the walk"* (now ten, with the near-miss recorded — `A→B→A` is not `A→A`) and defined *"A directly attests B"* without requiring them to differ; and `docs/sybil-cost-and-the-root-set.md`, the document 3.7 cites, costed the attack at K keys when **the candidate themselves was cheaper at zero** — its figures were an upper bound and now say so. **WHAT THIS UNBLOCKS:** 1.26's option (b) was priced by this row, and with self excluded a claim-to-claim `SynapticLink` no longer widens an attestation hole — so that option is now genuinely coordinator-only with no fork, which is the cheapest route 1.26 has. | ~~**D**~~ **DECIDED AND BUILT 2026-11-08** | nothing — coordinator-only, no DNA change, no fork (hash verified identical) |
| 1.28 | ~~**The applet view — Moss detects this UI as a Tool and gets a message saying it is not implemented.**~~ **BUILT 2026-11-10, and the implementation corrected three names this row took from documentation and found two defects nothing was watching for.** *Opened 2026-11-08 out of #450, which took the dependency and wired the branch.* **WHAT SHIPPED.** `AppletView`'s three arms each render a surface: `main` is `renderTabs()` — the whole app, unchanged — `asset` is one claim read with `get_claim`, and `creatable` is the same New Claim form with the tab bar removed and both halves of the host's dialog contract honoured. `RenderInfo` is consumed rather than detected: the host's group name is on screen, and the first `WeaveClient.connect()` is made. `HolochainConnection.fromAppClient` is a third connection shape (four lines, no socket, no token) which is what keeps this one client rather than two. **THE ROW'S OWN FOUR CONSTRAINTS ALL HELD, and two of them changed shape when the source was read.** (1) The standalone app is unaffected, measured in the same run rather than argued. (2) The gate rule is enforceable rather than remembered — `hostMayGate` names `publish`, `critique`, `retract` and `join` as REFUSED explicitly, because an empty allow-list would also pass a test and would say nothing about intent. (3) HRLs persisted, WAL urls refused — **and the guard had a hole**: it was anchored (`/^weave-\d+\.\d+:\/\//`) and `weaveUrlFromWal(wal, webPrefix)` returns `https://theweave.social/wal?weave-0.12://hrl/…`, so the host's own default shareable link passed straight through into a permanent entry. Un-anchoring it turns 3 checks red and the other 10 in that section stayed green throughout, which is how it survived. (4) **No WAL parser was needed at all**: a WAL is `{ hrl: [DnaHash, ActionHash|EntryHash], context? }` — already-decoded hashes, not a string — and the url direction is `weaveUrlToWAL`, the host's own function. **THREE NAMES IN THIS ROW WERE DOC-DERIVED AND WRONG, which is #450's lesson arriving on schedule.** `MossPrivilege`/`MossRole` exist but are not what a Tool is handed (`myAccountabilitiesPerGroup()` returns `MossAccountability[]` per group, async and plural); `pickAsset` is `client.assets.userSelectAsset(from?)` returning `WAL | undefined`; and `notifyCreated` did not exist at all — it was invented from a sentence, and declaring it hid that `view.resolve(wal)` is MANDATORY inside a creatable render while `notifyFrame` is optional. **TWO MEASURED DEFECTS, both in code nothing was watching.** First, **`WeaveClient.connect()` CAN NEVER RESOLVE**: with no `window.__WEAVE_RENDER_INFO__` it awaits an `applet-iframe-ready` event with no timeout and an unused `_reject` (`api.js:189`), and `isWeaveContext()` is true on the `applet:` protocol ALONE — so an unguarded await there is a permanently blank page with nothing in the console, strictly worse than the notice this row replaced. Hence `CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS` and a stated `applet-unavailable`. Second, **the cross-group arm carries no `appletClient` and its `view.type` is also the string `'main'`**, so switching on `view.type` alone renders the single-group view and then reads a client that is not there. Declined in words. **AND THE HARNESS FOUND A THIRD, IN THIS SESSION'S OWN NEW CODE:** the asset panel said *"Nothing came back for that address"* on its first frame, about a claim that was there, because `bootApplet` renders once before starting the read — **6.13's finding, unprompted, written by someone who had just read 6.13**. Caught only by the MutationObserver; all three checks that READ the panel were green. Fixed with a named `unasked` state, and `unreadable` split from `absent` on the same principle. **GATED BY TWO HARNESSES, and one of them was running nowhere at all before this:** `applet-prototype-check.mjs` (48 pure checks, registered in `ui.yml` for the first time) and `scripts/live-verify/applet-view.mjs` (42 browser checks against a FAKE Moss host whose `appletClient` bridges to a real conductor, so *a Tool render reaches real DHT data without touching the Admin API* is measured, the same way `launcher-packaging.mjs` measures the Launcher). **STILL UNPROVEN: a real Moss install.** None exists here, and the fake host is faithful only in the respect that matters — the client comes from outside the page and the page has no admin access. **COST, re-measured:** the view added 8,556 bytes to the bundle (679,703 → 688,259, +1.26%) on top of the 2,726 the dependency already cost. | ~~M~~ **BUILT 2026-11-10** | ~~nothing~~ **nothing — closed, and gated by `applet-prototype-check.mjs` and `applet-view.mjs` in `ui.yml`** |
| 1.29 | **Nine harnesses carrying 210 assertions are run by no workflow, and not one of them says so.** *Opened 2026-11-10, found while closing 1.28 — which fixed this exact defect in a tenth file and then asked whether there were others.* `mobile-ui/applet-prototype-check.mjs` shipped with #450, carried 14 checks, and appeared in no workflow at all for two days; it is registered now. **Sweeping for the same shape found nine more**, every one of which `process.exit(1)`s on a failure and is therefore a gate rather than an experiment: `network-partition` (26), `superior-egg` (33), `registry-install` (6), `index-lens` (15), `microbiome` (19), `proprioception` (34), `peer-signal` (19), `secretion` (24), `standing` (34). **THE DISTINCTION THAT MAKES THIS A DEFECT RATHER THAN A PREFERENCE.** This directory already has deliberately-unrun files and they ALL declare it in their own README row — `scale-dht`, `dht-isolation-probe`, `stall-bisect`, `stall-threshold`, `strategy-compare` and `clock-skew-reveal` each say **"an experiment, not a gate and not in CI"** and give the reason (twelve conductors would measure the runner; `libfaketime` moves a clock and does not belong on a shared machine). **None of the nine above says anything of the kind**, so a reader of `live-verify/README.md` cannot tell a gate that runs from a gate that does not, and the suite's own index reports 69 harnesses while CI invokes 25. **AND `check-harness-counts.mjs` CANNOT CATCH IT, which is why it needs a row rather than a fix.** That gate asserts the README's four index claims against what EXISTS — table rows, `CHROMIUM` importers, the suite count, and `ui.yml`'s own header against `ui.yml`'s own invocations. Every one of those is currently green. Nothing compares the set of harnesses to the set that any workflow runs, so a harness can be perfectly documented, perfectly counted, and never executed. **A gate that does not run is indistinguishable from one that was never written, except that it reads like coverage** — and this list exists to catch exactly that shape. **THE WORK IS A DECISION FIRST AND WIRING SECOND, and the decision is per harness, not global.** Some of the nine plainly cannot go in `ui.yml`: `network-partition` needs `scripts/netns.sh` and a real partition, and `registry-install` installs from a registry. Others look like ordinary single-conductor harnesses that were simply never wired. So the unit of work is: for each, either **register it** in the workflow whose setup it already needs, or **declare it manual in its README row with the reason**, in the words the six existing experiments use. The cheap, mechanical half worth doing either way is a **gate for the gate** — assert that every `check()`-bearing harness is either invoked by a workflow or carries a manual declaration, which is the only thing that stops this recurring. | **M** for the sweep and the new gate; **D** for whether a given harness belongs in CI at all, since three of the six existing exemptions are about what a shared runner can honestly measure | nothing — the harnesses exist and pass locally; `check-harness-counts.mjs` is the file the new assertion belongs in |
| 1.30 | **Moss delivers notifications for free, and 6.15's push half is open because the PROTOCOL's way of doing it is expensive — so inside Moss the cost that blocks that decision does not exist.** *Opened 2026-11-13 by auditing what 1.28 did NOT take.* **THE BLOCKER IT SIDESTEPS, restated so the two are not confused.** 6.15's `[D]` is push-versus-pull, and its cost is concrete: `send_peer_signal` writes a permanent CapGrant **per call on the SENDER's chain**, so notifying on every critique would grow a critic's own chain in proportion to how much they disagree — a permanent per-act cost on exactly the behaviour this protocol wants more of. **`WeaveClient.notifyFrame(notifications: Array<FrameNotification>)`** (`@theweave/api/dist/api.d.ts:267`) writes to no chain at all: Moss raises the notification in its own frame. `FrameNotification` is `{ title, body, notification_type, icon_src }` (`types.d.ts:43`), which is enough for *somebody critiqued your claim* and carries no ranking, no count and no ordering. **IT IS MENTIONED IN THIS CODEBASE AND NEVER CALLED** — `mobile-ui/src/applet.ts:340` names it in a comment describing the host surface, which is the same shape as `profilesClient` in 1.31: received, documented, dropped. **AND IT DOES NOT ANSWER 6.15, WHICH IS THE POINT OF FILING IT SEPARATELY RATHER THAN INSIDE IT.** This reaches a reader only when the app is running AS A MOSS TOOL. **Every pilot participant runs the standalone desktop build, so a notification built here reaches none of them**, and 6.15's `[D]` stands untouched for the only people currently using this protocol. What it does is make the Moss arm the place where push is cheap, which is worth knowing before anybody pays the CapGrant price to have it everywhere. **THE HONEST SHAPE OF THE WORK:** one call at the point `create_critique` succeeds on somebody else's claim, guarded on the applet arm being live — and a decision that is NOT 6.15's, namely whether a Tool should notify at all when the protocol deliberately does not. | M for the call, D for whether notifying is this client's business | nothing technical — but it is worth settling 6.15's `[D]` first, since building the cheap arm may quietly answer the expensive one by precedent |
| 1.31 | **The app is handed real agent profiles by Moss and drops them — which is a better answer to 6.23 than anything this client could mint, and reaches nobody in the pilot.** *Opened 2026-11-13 by the same audit.* **WHAT IS BEING DROPPED, read out of the installed types rather than the docs.** `RenderInfo` for an applet view carries **`profilesClient: ProfilesClient`** (`@theweave/api/dist/types.d.ts:107` and `:180`), and `WeaveClient` exposes **`appletParticipants(): Promise<AgentPubKey[]>`** (`api.d.ts:295`). `mobile-ui/src/applet.ts:243-244` names `profilesClient` **in a comment** documenting the shape and nothing consumes it. **THE CONTRAST THAT MAKES THIS A REAL GAP RATHER THAN A TIDY-UP:** the sibling field `groupProfiles` IS consumed — `applet-host.ts:215` reads `appletView?.groupProfiles?.[0]?.name` for the group label — so the GROUP has a name on screen and the PEOPLE in it do not. **WHY IT BEARS ON 6.23's `[D]`.** That row asks whether to mint a key-derived identity — a colour, a mnemonic, an identicon — because twelve base64 characters are not something a reader can compare, and it rules out a display NAME on the grounds that nothing in the protocol binds one to a key. **Moss binds one.** Inside a Moss group the nickname is the group's own, vouched for by the host rather than asserted by this client, so the Moss arm does not need to invent anything: it asks. **That is strictly better than an identicon where it is available, and available nowhere else.** **AND IT MUST NOT BE READ AS ANSWERING 6.23**, which is why this is its own row: the standalone desktop build has no profiles client, no group and no host to ask, and **that is the build every pilot participant runs**. A reader on the desktop app would still be comparing key prefixes. 6.23's `[M]` — *same author as an earlier critique* — already shipped and is the answer that reaches them; 6.23's `[D]` remains exactly as open as it was. **THE TRAP WORTH NAMING NOW:** a nickname is a thing people choose and can change, so a byline that says a name is making a weaker claim than a byline that says a key, and both on screen at once needs a rule about which one a critique is held to. That is not a styling question and it is not answered here. | M for the read, D for whether a host-supplied name may stand where a key stands | the `[D]`. **The read is unblocked** and is a handful of lines |
| 1.32 | **6.24 left a sub-decision open — a panel LEFT open goes stale — and Moss already carries the push side that would settle it without polling.** *Opened 2026-11-13 by the same audit.* **WHAT 6.24 SETTLED AND WHAT IT DID NOT.** Opening a critique panel now re-reads it, which covers the gesture a reader makes. What it does not cover is a panel somebody is already looking at: a critique published while they read it is invisible, and the two options the row named were **polling** — a continuous read nobody asked for — and **saying nothing**. **WHAT MOSS OFFERS INSTEAD**, read out of the installed types: `WeaveClient.sendRemoteSignal(payload: Uint8Array, toAgents?: AgentPubKey[])` (`api.d.ts:305`), `onRemoteSignal(callback)` (`:313`), and `onPeerStatusUpdate(callback)` (`:198`), with `peerStatusStore: ReadonlyPeerStatusStore` on `RenderInfo` itself (`types.d.ts:181`). **None of the four is referenced anywhere in `mobile-ui/src/`.** A remote signal is ephemeral and writes to no chain — it is not `send_peer_signal` and carries none of that extern's per-call CapGrant cost (see 1.30). **WHY THIS IS THE RIGHT SHAPE AND POLLING IS NOT:** a signal arrives exactly when something happened, so a panel refreshes on an event rather than on a timer, and a quiet claim costs nothing. Polling pays continuously to discover that nothing changed, which is the trade 6.24 declined to make blind. **AND THE PROTOCOL'S OWN SIGNAL IS NO HELP HERE, which 6.24 already established and is worth not re-deriving:** `SignalPayload::NewCritique` is declared at `dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs:5234` and **emitted nowhere**, and `emit_signal` reaches only the LOCAL conductor's websocket clients — so a critique published by a remote agent emits on their node and never arrives. Moss's remote signal is a different mechanism that actually crosses agents. **THE SAME CEILING AS 1.30 AND 1.31, AND IT IS THE REASON ALL THREE ARE SEPARATE ROWS RATHER THAN ONE: this reaches only the Moss arm, and every pilot participant runs the standalone desktop build.** 6.24's sub-`[D]` is therefore **not** answered by this and must not be closed by it — what changes is that the Moss arm has a third option that is neither polling nor silence. **A CAUTION ON SCOPE:** a live-updating panel is a surface changing under a reader while they decide what to say about it, which is the one thing §4.4 cares about most. Arriving critiques appearing silently mid-read is a different defect from stale ones, not an improvement on them — so the useful version of this probably marks that something arrived rather than re-rendering underneath them. | M for the wiring, D for whether a panel may change while it is being read | the `[D]`, which is 6.24's sub-decision seen from the other side |

## 2. The autopoiesis staging — in dependency order, not preference order

**COMPLETE as of 2026-10-24.** All five stages are on `main`: the medium (#318), proprioception (#319), standing (#324), secretion (#320) and the microbiome (#321). Every one is coordinator-only — no entry type, no link type, no validation rule — so the DNA hash is untouched and nothing here forked the network.

The ordering is forced, not chosen. Stage 1 exists to make Stage 2 affordable;
Stage 2 is the only item that decides whether **operational closure** is true
rather than aspirational; Stage 3 is the first *consequence*; 4 and 5 are
downstream of both.

| # | item | who | blocked on |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | ~~**Stage 1 — the medium.**~~ Typed ephemeral peer signals. One entry to open a channel, zero per signal. Coordinator-only and **proven not to fork**: the DNA hash was byte-identical before and after **CLOSED 2026-10-23 (#318).** One entry to open a channel, zero per signal, four typed variants round-tripping, 22 checks. | — | done |
| 2.2 | ~~**Stage 2 — proprioception.**~~ `get_local_tension`: one read bundling closure deficit, ordering ambiguity, friction headroom, conductance decay, arc width. Then dispositions respond to it. **This is the stage that matters.** Luhmann's criterion for an autopoietic system of communications is that a communication be produced by prior communications rather than by the environment; right now every claim is produced by a human or an externally-triggered agent, and this is the single change that makes the graph's own state the proximate cause of the next write **CLOSED 2026-10-23 (#319).** `get_local_tension`, caller-scoped **by construction** — no agent parameter, so it cannot become the per-agent scalar Invariant 1 bars. 34 checks, four injections. **Four signals, not five: arc width is unreachable from a zome**, and the gap is reported as `signals_bundled: 4 / signals_intended: 5` rather than hidden. | — | done |
| 2.3 | ~~**Stage 3 — standing / apoptosis.**~~ Standing as a function of causal distance, computed at read time, writing nothing. Primitives exist (`order_records`, `past_cone`, `get_grounding_path`). Needs a harness with injections, since a standing rule that cannot go red is this repository's recurring failure **CLOSED 2026-10-24 (#324) WITHOUT waiting on 3.1**, by making the threshold the caller's promise. Apoptosis not necrosis: reported, still fetchable by hash, never deleted. Counted in causal **edges**, never ordinal position, since `tie_broken` means ordinal distance is part hash. 26 checks, five injections. **3.1 still decides whether a membrane may DECLARE N** — an addition to this read, not a replacement. | — | done |
| 2.4 | ~~**Stage 4 — secretion.**~~ Dispositions propose `CritiqueSpecies`; `get_species_adoption` read a second time **at a causal distance from the first**, which is the only way exploration becomes distinguishable from degradation. The swarm currently passes `species: null` (`scripts/swarm/run-swarm.mjs:250`) **CLOSED 2026-10-23 (#320).** Uptake told from a private vocabulary by **causal distance** from `past_cone`, not by polling twice — two reads are separated by a clock and §5.22 rules that out. A live 3-agent run produced both classes: 1 downstream adopter at depth 1 on `unevidenced-claim`, all-proposer citations on `method-unstated`. | — | done |
| 2.5 | ~~**Stage 5 — microbiome.**~~ Membranes read each other's published policies and import foreign critiques. `federation/` currently contains **zero** references to `ConductancePolicy` or `AttestationPolicy` **CLOSED 2026-10-23 (#321).** A foreign critique is a **witness** under the importer's own key — §5.2 binds author to the action author, so writing somebody else's objection would forge provenance. Excluded from `get_discourse_health` and counted as `imported_claims`, or importing fifty would make a domain's health a reading of another network. Verified across two genuinely separate DNA hashes. **`ForeignCritique` as a real entry type remains §4's bump.** | — | done |

## 3. Research questions — no code until these have answers

| # | question | who | why it is open |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3.1 | ~~**What sets the atrophy threshold?**~~ Quantitative Promise Theory: a threshold turning a signal into a decision **is itself a promise and needs an agent behind it** — the argument that retired `CONDUCTANCE_HALF_LIFE_SECS` as a universal constant with no promiser. **DECIDED 2026-10-24: THE READER IS THE PROMISER, and the answer was already shipped.** `StandingPolicy::max_causal_distance` (Stage 3, #324) makes the threshold the CALLER's promise. The row offered two candidates — per-membrane **declared** or **derived** — and a third had appeared since it was written. The deciding property is that the caller-supplied form has **a neutral off position**: `None` means no atrophy, not a default, so Invariant #1's "constant with no promiser" objection is answered in full rather than half-answered as it had to be for a half-life, where time passes whether or not anybody promised a rate. **Per-reader is enough; no membrane-wide answer is wanted.** Consequences: **4.4 leaves the §4 bump** (a declared-N `Constitution` field is not being built), and **3.2 loses its driver** — see its row. | — | done |
| 3.2 | **Is there a derived measure that does not punish exploration?** Project 89's dead-head criterion has exactly the right *form* — `τ = 0.96/√d`, local to the component, zero parameters, validated against ablation ground truth at 95-100% across six models. But it equates low alignment with the receiver's state with death, and in a discourse graph the orthogonal contribution is sometimes the only valuable one. **The swarm deliberately runs an exploring minority that this criterion would classify as dead on sight.** Candidates to replace alignment: grounding density, citation closure, assessment reachability over causal distance **NO LONGER BLOCKS ANYTHING, 2026-10-24.** Its blocker read *"3.1's second branch depends on it"*, and 3.1 closed per-reader — so the derived branch is not being pursued and nothing waits on this. **Kept as an open research question rather than closed**, because the thing it actually asks is still unanswered and still interesting: whether a measure can be derived that does not classify the exploring minority as dead on sight. **AND IT NOW HAS A WORKED EXAMPLE IN SOMEBODY ELSE'S DOMAIN, which is what it was missing — `docs/cases/luck-light-egg.md` §13.7**, written because Luck Light asked for one: a membrane arguing about milligrams of choline per egg (methodology, sample sizes, lab variance), and one buyer who says *your number is fine, the weak link is your feed supplier's certification chain.* **Aligned with nothing around it, so an alignment-keyed criterion scores it dead on arrival — and it is also the only critique in the thread that could cost the seller a contract.** The example is worth more than the abstraction: it names what the measure must NOT do in a case where the stakes are legible, and it is the version to hand to anybody designing the simulations 7.15 accepted. Reopen it if a membrane-wide or derived threshold is ever wanted. **MEASURED 2026-11-10, AND THE ANSWER IS NEGATIVE — `scripts/live-verify/exploration-measures.mjs`, 12 checks, which builds §13.7 on a real conductor instead of reasoning about it.** An experiment rather than a gate and declared as one, because §3 records which way the protocol behaves rather than requiring it. **(1) GROUNDING DENSITY, THIS ROW'S OWN FIRST CANDIDATE, FAILS FOR THE SAME REASON ALIGNMENT DOES.** Fixture: the choline claim with a lab report behind it, an ALIGNED methodology critique citing a counter-study, the ORTHOGONAL *your feed supplier's certification chain* critique citing nothing, and a claim nobody engages. Measured: the orthogonal critique and the unengaged claim **both score zero**, so a grounding-keyed measure cannot tell them apart — while the claim everyone is arguing about is grounded, so the key is not simply broken. The buyer who spots a certification gap has no study to cite because the point is **structural rather than empirical**, and a measure reading *cites nothing* as *contributes nothing* kills exactly the contribution §13.7 was written to protect. **(2) AND A ZERO-PARAMETER GROUNDING CRITERION CANNOT EXIST.** Zero cited is `0/0`, not zero: substitute 0 and every structural critique is dead on arrival, substitute 1 and every unevidenced assertion is maximally alive. **The substitution IS the parameter**, and having none is the whole of `τ = 0.96/√d`'s claim to authority. **(3) THE THIRD CANDIDATE IS ALREADY BUILT AND DOES NOT MEAN WHAT THIS ROW ASSUMED — a control caught the mistake rather than prose.** The first draft of that section asserted that a tight `max_causal_distance` atrophies the unengaged claim and spares the argued one. It does **neither**. `engagement_distance` is the HEIGHT of the shallowest record referencing a claim, and with nothing referencing it the coordinator uses `heights[idx]` — the claim's own depth — with `own_depth_used: true` saying so. So *nobody engaged it* scores as **as fresh as its own position**. Swept over thresholds 0, 1, 2, 3 and 5: **nothing atrophies at any of them**, with `own_depth_used` (false for the argued claim, true for the lonely one) as the control proving the report does distinguish the two cases, just not through `has_standing`. **So reachability over causal distance is an ENGAGEMENT-DEPTH REPORT, not a dead-head criterion** — it needs causal depth to say anything, and §13.7's egg membrane is exactly the flat shape most disagreements have while they are still live. It does not punish exploration, and it does not punish anything else here either. **(4) SO THE QUESTION HAS NO POSITIVE ANSWER IN THIS PROTOCOL AS BUILT, AND THE REASON IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN A LACK OF CLEVERNESS.** No measure here separates *orthogonal and valuable* from *nobody engaged* — and **Invariant 1 is why nobody has built one**: its own note says a hardcoded, always-applied version of a caller-supplied policy *"would itself be the kind of canonical signal this invariant rules out"*, so **any protocol-DEFAULT threshold is forbidden whatever it is keyed to.** Changing the key from alignment to grounding does not rescue it; it changes which minority it kills. The repository already hit this exact wall once — `CONDUCTANCE_HALF_LIFE_SECS = 30 days` was precisely this shape, one constant over every membrane converting a signal into a decision with no promiser — and the resolution was not a better constant but **moving the threshold to the reader**. The exploring minority is protected by there being no default at all. **(5) AND THE LIVE REMAINDER IS AN ASYMMETRY, NOT A MISSING MEASURE.** The two read-layer atrophy measures answer *what does a reader who promised nothing get* differently: `get_standing` takes the strict reading (`None => true`, nothing dies, and `standing.mjs` already gates it), while `get_effective_conductance` applies thirty days regardless. **SPEC §7's own note on Invariant 1 admits this half survives** — *"a reader who supplies no policy still gets thirty days, still with no promiser"* — so one existing default has the shape `τ = 0.96/√d` has and the shape the note rules out. **That is the actionable thing this row leaves behind, and it is a decision rather than research:** either conductance takes the strict reading too (no promiser, no decay — a change to what every existing conductance read returns, explicitly not taken on 2026-10-19) or the asymmetry is recorded as deliberate. **STAYS OPEN**, because (5) is undecided and because a measure keyed to something nobody has proposed yet is not ruled out by any of this — but it is open with three candidates retired and a reason rather than with an abstraction. | **M** — ~~research~~ **the measurement is DONE 2026-11-10; what remains in (5) is D** | nothing — research, not a blocker. **(5) needs the human's decision on whether conductance takes the strict reading, which is a change to every existing conductance read** |

| 3.9 | **Remedy has no home in this protocol, and Promise Theory says where it has to live: in some named agent's own promise.** *Opened 2026-10-27 out of the egg case's fourth exchange — `docs/cases/luck-light-egg.md` §13.6.* **THE QUESTION**: what is the cheapest structure that gives a harmed party a counterparty, without a centre, without personhood, and without this protocol asserting anything it cannot? **WHY IT IS NOT AN ENGINEERING ROW**: *you cannot make a promise on another agent's behalf*, so no protocol delivers remedy by itself — which means this is a question about what promise structure to RECOGNISE, not about what to compute. **THE SHAPE, and it is Mattereum's, recorded as an influence in `README` §2.4 while only the witness half was implemented** (*"not a Ricardian contract in the strict sense — no human-signed agreement, no legal enforceability, and no obligation"*): an underwriter publishes a `Constitution` promising to make a claimant whole; a `Membrane` makes that a **required promise** for claims of a given kind; a reader's policy declines claims that carry none; **6.6's read already shows who actually promised what**. (2) **7.16's residual risk**: the people most likely to be harmed never signed the joining promise (7.17), and a remedy promise is the only structure on this list that reaches them. (3) **The sharpest formulation the egg case produced**: §11.1 says a challengeable claim is one that says what would make it wrong; **a warranty adds who pays when it is.** **THE HONEST LIMITS.** Enforcement happens OUTSIDE the DHT, in a jurisdiction somebody chooses — this protocol can record that a promise was made and point at who made it, and it cannot compel payment, which is the half 7.16's advice and `README` §2.4 both already say is not here. An underwriter is also a concentration of trust by construction; what keeps it from being a CENTRE is that it is named per claim and chosen per reader, never a protocol default (Invariant 1's note). And pricing requires loss data nobody has. **Related**: 3.7 (the scarce resource), 3.4 (why a running balance is not available), 7.16 (the exposure), 7.17 (what a joining promise cannot reach), 4.6 (what a withdrawable claim does to a warranty — an unresolved interaction worth stating before either is built). | **D** to adopt any of it; M to cost it as research | nothing — and 7.17's adoption is the first place it would show up |
| 3.10 | **Monoculture is 3.5's unverifiable cost, and `PreRegistration`/`Revelation` can measure it — but not in the form the idea arrived in.** *Opened 2026-10-31 from the 2026-10-29 AI review, which named it fifth and framed it as inverting AI's weakness into a detector.* 3.5 files observer diversity as unverifiable: ten identities may be one actor, and ten observers on one model are one mind in different hats. **The sealing mechanism makes correlation observable where accuracy is not.** §5.22's MUST forbids deriving an accuracy figure because `reveal_deadline` is forgeable by clock skew; the **content** binding is airtight (the author must be the committer and the content must hash to the sealed commitment), so correlation across revealed content uses only the sound half of the mechanism and the MUST is untouched. **THREE PROBLEMS, AND THE FIRST TWO ARE THE REASON THIS IS A RESEARCH ROW RATHER THAN AN ENGINEERING ONE.** (1) **High correlation is not monoculture when the question is easy.** Two genuinely independent observers predicting the same obvious outcome correlate perfectly. Raw correlation would therefore fire hardest exactly where it means least, so the measure needs a baseline — correlation conditioned on the spread of revealed outcomes, or restricted to questions where the outcomes actually diverged — and that baseline is the whole research question, not an implementation detail. (2) **Selection is a second channel, not noise.** Agents choose what to pre-register, so two agents correlate partly because they chose the same targets; target choice is itself observable and measurable, but it is a distinct signal and conflating it with content correlation would overstate both. (3) **THE DETECTOR'S POPULATION AND ITS SUBJECT ARE THE SAME POPULATION.** The idea's own argument is that only AI agents can generate pre-registrations in volume — which reintroduces precisely the condition being measured. **And 3.5 records that this repository has already bought the defence it would be testing for:** swarm dispositions are *mechanical*, every rule a property of auditable data, so the variation does not come from a model and ten observers genuinely differ on one model. **So the condition this row detects does not currently exist by design.** It becomes live exactly when 4.2 is adopted — observers-as-entries makes the selected thing a prompt and throws that defence away, which 3.5 already names — or when model-driven participants join a real membrane. ## 4. Behind one integrity fork — accumulate, then bump once

Every edit to the integrity zome forks the network, additive changes included,
so these batch into a single `protocol_version` bump rather than four. **Stages
1 to 3 need no fork at all**, which is why they come first.

**The candidate set is five, and this section listed three until 2026-10-24 and
four until 2026-10-26 night.**
`docs/integrity-bump-plan.md` §2 is the authority on the set and says so in its
own heading: 4.1-4.3 below, plus **4.5**, which existed only in the plan, and
now **4.6**, added the night 7.16 got its advice. 4.4 —
a declared atrophy threshold `N` — was added and dropped on the same day, when
3.1 decided the reader is the promiser; it is not listed because it is dead,
not pending. **The gap is the lesson**: a candidate added in a plan document
does not reach the ordered list by itself.

| # | item | who | blocked on |
|---|---|---|---|
| 4.1 | **The foresight ordinal anchor.** `PreRegistration.reveal_deadline` changes type to a position in a declared order; `Revelation` gains an association; `Membrane` gains a declared notary key. Validation then checks shape only — the association resolves, its author is the declared notary, its counter precedes the deadline's. **Not adopted** — the 2026-10-19 decision stands, since no surface grants this timestamp authority — but the reopen order changed: repair 3 first because it is free, then **repair 4**, and countersigning only if an ordinal anchor is unavailable **THE QUESTIONS ARE ASKED (7.6, sent). This now waits on ANSWERS, not on anybody writing an email** — which is a different blocker and outside this repository's control. Nothing else in §4 depends on them, so the bump is not held up by 4.1 alone. | D | Andras's three answers |
| 4.2 | **Observers as entries**, if adopted — prompts, heuristics and thresholds as signed entries, so selection acts on the agents. Contrary to one claim on file, this **is** a new entry type and therefore does fork **AND ITS COST IS NOW MEASURED RATHER THAN ARGUED, 2026-11-08.** 3.5's *accidental defence* — that dispositions are mechanical, so ten observers differ even on one model — is enumerated and enforced by `scripts/swarm/check-dispositions-mechanical.mjs`: **the swarm's whole behaviour is 60 states, deterministic, with the majority/minority divergence confined to exactly 4 of them, all inside the declared threshold band.** Replacing a threshold with a prompt discards every one of those properties, and the gate would have nothing left to check. **So this row's decision is now: is selection over prompts worth losing an enumerable, attributable swarm?** — a priced question rather than an open-ended one. That workaround was chosen precisely to avoid a fork and carries a real cost: a reader must filter by tag, and `get_discourse_health` needed `imported_claims` to keep its own count honest. One new entry type, **appended** — entry types are position-indexed, so it cannot go anywhere else. See `docs/integrity-bump-plan.md` §2. | ~~[M] once the bump is cut~~ **WRITTEN AND HELD: PR #350, draft** | ~~the bump itself, which is **[D]**~~ — **[D] TAKEN 2026-10-25: the bump goes AFTER inviting ten people.** So this waits on the batch, not on a decision: 4.1 (Andras), 4.2 (3.5 priced), 4.3 (3.4 settled). | 4.6 | **Per-field content mode: `public_permanent` or `withdrawable`, declared by the membrane.** *Opened 2026-10-26 night, out of 7.16's advice — `docs/erasure-and-the-content-split.md` §3.* Readable content moves off the shared network (or onto it encrypted, §3.2 there) while **the hash, the signature, the order and the links stay** — which is Invariant 6's operative reading (§5.22: *no agent may erase another's record of what was promised*) left intact, because that reading is about the RECORD and not the CONTENT. **IT IS NOT A NEW PATTERN HERE: `SealedPrediction` already is this**, and `dna/integrity/src/lib.rs:798-810` writes down both halves — the Action publishes the hash so the author cannot quietly change what they committed to, and **validation cannot see a private entry's body at all** (hdi returns `None`, `Some(_)` is an error). **WHY IT FORKS**: new fields on `Claim` at minimum, plausibly a new entry type for the custodial-key variant, so it batches with 4.1-4.5 rather than going before the invitation. **THE COST IS INVENTORIED RATHER THAN ESTIMATED — four content-inspecting rules exist in the whole integrity zome** (`validate_claim`'s non-empty content `:1331`, `validate_mew`'s 560 chars `:1309`, `SettlementPolicy` name/statement `:1513`/`:1516`, `WorldlineTrace`'s 64KB `:2119`), and only the first two are personal content; both are shape rules that move author-side. That is §5.22's family — a goal no deterministic rule over reachable data can express — joined for the fourth time, not a new kind of problem. **THE PER-FIELD GRANULARITY IS THE WHOLE DESIGN**: *"batch 47: 410mg choline"* stays public and permanent in Luck Light's egg membrane, which **requires** that mode by promise, while a farmer's name and address stay withdrawable — so the objection this risks (`docs/cases/luck-light-egg.md:390`, *a track record with the bad quarters removed is not a track record*) does not apply to the case that raised it. **The custodial variant needs 4.1's ordinal anchor for a SECOND purpose** — *erased as of version N* has to be an ordered, agreed event, and Andras's work supplies the ordering while key destruction supplies the erasure; that reading is second-hand and wants confirming with him. Honest limits in §5 of the doc: nothing erases a copy someone kept, and encryption-as-erasure is legally debated. | **D** to adopt; M to build once adopted | the bump batch, and the adoption decision — nothing legal, which is the change |
