The standing lesson held again: for any check of the form *X is NOT shown*, ask what makes it capable of failing. | ~~M for the write, D for where a reply renders~~ **BOTH DONE 2026-11-12 — the `[D]` answered by the participant, the `[M]` BUILT** | ~~the `[D]`~~ **nothing — closed, and gated by `reading-a-thread.mjs` in `ui.yml`** |
| 6.20 | **Permanent prose was collapsed on the way in and composed three lines at a time: every line break in a published entry was thrown away by CSS, and the box you write one in was `min-height: 5rem`.** *Opened 2026-11-11 by two more sentences from the same report: "The window to write in could be made bigger, right now wanting to read back (as it is permanent) is difficult" and "formatting, right now reading such a blurb becomes unpleasant."* **MEASURED BEFORE IT WAS FIXED, AND THE SHAPE OF THE MISS IS THE INTERESTING PART.** `white-space` appeared NINE times in 1,857 lines of stylesheet, three of them `pre-wrap`, and not one of those three was on a surface that renders a published entry's free text: they were the 6.17 window added hours earlier, plus two notes-layer surfaces, which are device-local drafts rather than permanent records. `textContent` had always put the whole string on screen and CSS had always run it together. **BUILT 2026-11-11 AS A LIST OF SURFACES RATHER THAN THE TWO THAT WERE REPORTED** — claim content, critique content, the retraction banner, an antibody flag and an assessment's rationale — and the injection that justifies the list is the one applying `pre-wrap` to ONLY the two complained about: exactly 1 red, the assessment rationale. Measured on screen: the same words published twice, once with blank lines and once with spaces, render **5 line boxes against 2**. NOT markdown, deliberately — rendering `*` and `#` would mean the bytes on the DHT and the claim on screen are no longer the same text, and every client would have to agree about a dialect for a permanent entry to read the same way twice. **AND THE BOX NOW FOLLOWS WHAT IS IN IT.** `fitProseBox` is delegated and app-wide rather than per form, for the reason `captureFieldValues` is: a rule that must be remembered at eleven call sites will be missing from the twelfth. Empty 136px, 297px for five paragraphs, capped at 464px of an 844px viewport where it scrolls instead — because a fixed-tall box pushes the button that commits a permanent entry off a phone screen, which is `layout-fits.mjs`'s defect wearing the other shoe, and removing the cap measured **9,294px** in that viewport. **EVERY CHECK HERE MEASURES LAYOUT, NEVER A COMPUTED STYLE:** a check that reads `white-space` off CSS asserts that a line was typed, not that a reader can see a paragraph, and it passes against an element the rule never reaches — which is exactly how `.critique-item p` was missed. | ~~M~~ **BUILT 2026-11-11** | ~~nothing~~ **nothing — closed and gated by `reading-a-thread.mjs`** |
| 6.21 | **The update button did nothing, and every way an update could fail was built to fail silently — one of them by stopping the app from starting.** *Opened 2026-11-12 by the pilot's first publisher, in two sentences: "App shows a window stating new version available. Install and restart. Clicking this - does nothing. No update happens."* **THE FIRST READING WAS WRONG, AND THE TRAP IS WORTH RECORDING BECAUSE IT WAS CONVINCING.** `gh release view v0.3.4` in the desktop repo listed assets named `org.holochain.kangaroo-electron-test-0.3.4-*` while all three `latest*.yml` manifests name `org.stateofintent.epistemic-resonance-0.3.4-*` — an exact manifest/asset mismatch, which would 404 every download on every platform and explain the report completely. **It is not real.** That repository has an `upstream` remote pointing at `holochain/kangaroo-electron`, the template it was made from, which publishes its own identically-numbered releases; with no default repo set, `gh` resolved the query there. Asked through `gh api repos/StateofIntent/...` the real assets match the manifests exactly, and a ranged GET on each manifest-named URL returns 206 while the kangaroo-named one returns 404. **A tooling default silently answered about a different project's release, and the answer was coherent.** `gh repo set-default` is the fix; reading `gh api` with an explicit path is the habit. **WHAT IS ACTUALLY WRONG, THREE DEFECTS, ALL OURS.** (1) **NOTHING WAS EVER LOGGED ANYWHERE A HUMAN COULD REACH.** `electron-updater` defaults its logger to `console` (`AppUpdater.js:142`) and the only `error` listener it registers is its own constructor's, whose entire body is `this._logger.error(...)`. `autoUpdater.logger` was never set, so in a packaged app every failure went to a stdout belonging to no terminal — **measured: the profile log file that "Export logs" ships contained zero lines matching `update`.** So the participant could not tell us what failed and we could not ask. (2) **NOTHING WAS EVER SHOWN TO THE USER AFTER THEY ACCEPTED.** A rejected `downloadUpdate()` was indistinguishable from a download still in progress, and both were indistinguishable from a button wired to nothing. (3) **AND THE UNGUARDED `await` ALSO ABORTED STARTUP.** `await autoUpdater.downloadUpdate()` sat bare inside the `app.whenReady()` callback, and the `launch()` that starts the conductor comes *after* it in the same callback — so a rejection there did not merely skip the update, it left the app on its splash screen forever. **The one case where somebody most needs a working app — they accepted an update that then failed — was the case where it would not start.** A fourth thing is not a defect but is half the report: the download is **140–200MB** and the dialog promised a restart without mentioning that anything had to be fetched first, so *"does nothing"* was also the honest reading of a download working normally. **THE PLATFORM IS ANSWERED: macOS. They replied "I am on a MAC" on 2026-11-12**, which promotes the suspicion below from a reading of the source to the right place to look — and settles it without anybody sending a log, which matters because the log did not exist. **SO THE LEADING CAUSE IS NOW THE ONE THIS ROW ALREADY NAMED:** `macOSCodeSigning: false`, `MacUpdater` hands the zip to Squirrel.Mac through a local proxy server, and Squirrel will not swap a bundle it cannot verify. **STILL NOT MEASURED, and the distinction is the whole discipline of this row:** nobody has yet watched that refusal happen, `nativeUpdater.on("error")` routed to the same invisible logger the fix replaces, and there is no Mac here to drive. What the fix buys is that the NEXT report arrives with the answer attached rather than requiring one. **WHAT WAS KNOWN BEFORE THEY ANSWERED, and is unchanged:** **Linux/AppImage was driven end to end on 2026-11-12 and updates correctly** — the shipped `v0.3.3` AppImage was run, offered 0.3.4, downloaded it, replaced its own file in `~/Downloads` and relaunched as 0.3.4. So the failure is macOS or Windows. **macOS is the suspect and only the suspect:** `macOSCodeSigning: false`, `MacUpdater` hands the zip to Squirrel.Mac via a local proxy, and Squirrel will not swap a bundle it cannot verify — but its `nativeUpdater.on("error")` routes to the same invisible `_logger`, so nobody has ever seen that error and suspecting is not measuring. **Windows signature verification is ruled OUT by reading the artifact rather than the docs:** `NsisUpdater.verifySignature` returns `null` early when `publisherName` is absent from `app-update.yml` (`NsisUpdater.js:84-99`), and the shipped `app-update.yml` has only `owner`, `repo`, `provider` and `updaterCacheDirName`. **THE FIX, BUILT 2026-11-12 in `epistemic-resonance-desktop`.** `logs.ts` hoists the rotating file transport out of `setupLogs` and exports `createUpdaterLogger`, so the updater writes `label: "UPDATER"` lines into the same file "Export logs" ships; `const.ts` exports `RELEASES_URL`, **read out of `app-update.yml` itself** so the download page a failure offers cannot drift from the repository the update was offered out of; and the whole flow moves into `offerUpdateIfAvailable()`, wrapped in `try/catch` at the call site so no update failure can stop the app starting. A failure now raises a dialog naming it, offering the download page, and saying that installing by hand keeps the data, the key and the network. The offer dialog says the size and that the app restarts itself. Progress goes on the taskbar/dock item via `setProgressBar` and into the log. **A check that cannot reach GitHub stays silent on purpose** — that is the ordinary offline start, and the `error` listener is therefore registered only *after* the user has asked for the update, since the updater emits `error` for a failed check too (`AppUpdater.js:230`). **MEASURED ON A PACKAGED BUILD, INCLUDING THE FAILURE, WHICH IS THE PART THAT WOULD OTHERWISE BE A VACUOUS GREEN.** An `electron-builder --linux dir` build was run with `APPIMAGE` set so `AppImageUpdater.isUpdaterActive()` passes, and its `resources/app-update.yml` repointed at a local `python3 -m http.server` serving a `latest-linux.yml` that advertises a 0.3.5 whose file does not exist. Driving the real dialog needed `hyprctl dispatch 'hl.dsp.send_shortcut{mods="", key="Tab", window="title:Update Available"}'` then the same with `key="space"` — **Return is useless for this, because GTK fires the DEFAULT button regardless of focus, which is `Deny`, and that mistake silently measured the decline path twice.** Results: the 404 arrived, **both** failure routes logged to the file (the rejected promise and the `error` event, two lines) while **one** dialog appeared — *"The update could not be installed"* — and after dismissing it **the app launched to its main window**, which is defect (3) shown fixed rather than argued fixed. The no-update path logs three `UPDATER` lines where there were none. **THERE IS NO CI GATE AND THIS ROW DOES NOT PRETEND OTHERWISE:** the desktop repo has no test script and no test file anywhere, and its two workflows only build. The harness above is written down here so it can be repeated by hand. **AND THE ROW IS NOT CLOSED BY SHIPPING IT, FOR A REASON THAT IS THE WHOLE SHAPE OF THE BUG: A FIX TO THE UPDATER CANNOT BE DELIVERED BY THE UPDATER.** Whatever is released next, every existing install must take by hand — this participant included — so what closes this row is a participant successfully taking an *automatic* update, and nothing before that. | ~~M~~ **BUILT 2026-11-12 — unreleased, and closed only by a participant taking an automatic update** | **nothing for the build. The cause on macOS/Windows is unmeasured, and now measurable** |
| 6.22 | **A claim with many critiques is a wall of them, and the participant living with one asked for it to fold.** *Opened 2026-11-12 by the last sentence of the same message: "I'd include an option if possible in where the list minimizes after 3-4 critiques but can be opened to see all, to not clutter?"* **FILED SEPARATELY FROM 6.19 RATHER THAN INSIDE IT, on this file's own precedent — 6.16 was split out of 6.12 for exactly this reason: it is the unblocked half and goes invisible where it sits.** This is a presentation default over a list that already loads in full, so it needs no protocol change, no new extern and no decision; 6.19 is a write that does not exist yet. **IT SHOULD STILL BE BUILT AFTER 6.19, because the things it folds are the sections 6.19 creates** — folding a flat list and folding per-critique sections are different pieces of work, and doing it first means doing it twice. **THE ONE THING TO GET RIGHT, and it is this project's recurring trap rather than a styling note: a collapsed list must say how many it is hiding.** A fold that reads as the end of the list is the Worldline snapshot's defect again (6.16) and the under-count's again (0.3.3) — a surface that looks complete while omitting something. *"3 more critiques"* is the whole requirement; a chevron alone is not. **AND A CONSTRAINT FROM THE SAME PERSON, 2026-11-12, WHICH BOUNDS THIS ROW RATHER THAN MOTIVATING IT:** *"As long as anyone looking can see some information without having to click too much."* So the fold is a cap on a LONG list, never a default that hides a short one — a claim with two critiques must show both, and the threshold they named (3-4) is a floor on what stays visible rather than a target to collapse toward. **This also rules out folding the replies 6.19 renders**, which arrive unasked and already-visible for exactly this reason: a reply behind a click is the defence being harder to find than the objection. **AND THE COUNT IT PRINTS IS NOT A TALLY OF ANYTHING RANKED** — it is the length of a list the reader is already looking at, which is what keeps it clear of Invariant 1, the same line By Author walks when it declines to print a score. **BUILT 2026-11-12, AND THE THRESHOLD IS THE TOP OF THE RANGE THEY NAMED RATHER THAN THE BOTTOM.** `FOLD_CRITIQUES_AFTER = 4`: a claim with four critiques shows four and renders no control at all, because this is a CAP ON A LONG LIST and not a default that tidies a short one away — so the bias belongs on the side of showing more, which is their second sentence applied to their first. **THE SLICE IS OF TOP-LEVEL CRITIQUES ONLY, AND THE REPLIES 6.19 ADDED ARE NEVER FOLDED.** A visible critique keeps every reply it has: a reply behind a click is the defence being harder to find than the objection, which is the asymmetry 6.19 exists to remove and would be a poor thing to reintroduce one row later. **THE LABEL SAYS WHAT IT HIDES, INCLUDING THE REPLIES — *"Show 2 more critiques and 1 reply"*.** Counting only the critiques would understate the fold by exactly the amount this row is about, since a hidden critique takes its replies down with it. **AND THE REPLY COUNT IS OMITTED RATHER THAN GUESSED WHEN IT IS NOT KNOWN:** `repliesByCritique.get(...)` is `undefined` for a critique nobody has read replies for, so the clause appears only when EVERY hidden critique has been read — 6.13's unasked-is-not-zero applied to a number instead of a screen. **NOTHING IS RANKED AND NOTHING IS COUNTED PER AGENT.** The figure is the length of a list the reader is already looking at; the order is still the DHT's; and the By Author tab's refusal to print a per-agent tally is about a different object and does not reach here. **GATED AS SECTION 7 of `reading-a-thread.mjs` — 47 checks to 56 — and the control comes FIRST because it is the half most likely to regress:** with three critiques on screen there must be NO fold control at all, since a client that folds every list would pass every other check in the section. **THREE INJECTIONS, EACH RED EXACTLY WHERE IT SHOULD BE.** A bare chevron with no number: **2 red**. Folding every list including the short one: **1 red**, the control above. Counting the critiques and staying silent about the hidden reply: **1 red**, and only that one. **AND THE STALE-BUNDLE TRAP BIT AGAIN, IN THE FORM THIS FILE ALREADY RECORDS.** The first injection run reported a clean green: `npm run build` had failed on `TS6133` (a variable the injection left unused), `vite` never ran, and the harness drove the PREVIOUS bundle — a passing injection, which means *the check is insensitive or the injection is wrong*, and here it was neither. The runner used for the remaining injections refuses to start when the build is non-zero. **A SEPARATE PRE-EXISTING BEHAVIOUR WAS FOUND AND IS NOT FIXED HERE, DELIBERATELY:** the panel caches a claim's critiques for the session (`if (!critiquesByClaim.has(key))` in the toggle handler), so a critique published after a reader first opened that panel never appears in it until a reload — collapsing and re-opening renders the cached list. That is why section 7 reloads the page rather than re-opening the panel. It predates this row; **it is now 6.24, written 2026-11-13**, and the detail lives there. It is recorded here because the harness had to work around it and the workaround would otherwise look arbitrary. **AND IT WENT RED ON A RUNNER IN A HARNESS THAT IS NOT THIS ROW'S — WHICH IS THE GATE SET DOING ITS JOB, AND THE MOST USEFUL THING THAT HAPPENED HERE.** `onboarding-ui.mjs` asserts README §4.4's boundary — *chrome may adapt, the artifact under evaluation must not* — and did it by counting: its fixture seeds FIVE critiques and it checked that five render. The fold shows four, so a row about decluttering reddened a gate about the shared referent, locally green and only caught in CI. **THE QUESTION IT FORCED IS WHETHER THE FOLD VIOLATES §4.4, AND THE ANSWER IS NO — read against the section's own words rather than against the harness's proxy for them.** §4.4 requires that *"claim text, its critiques, their `CritiqueMode`s, authorship and ordering should render identically for every viewer"* and permits *"navigation, layout, information density, onboarding, theming and progressive disclosure"* to adapt freely. What it forbids is a PER-USER presentation, because that *"breaks this silently — the cross-check degrades and nobody can see that it has"*. **This fold is identical for every viewer, says on screen what it is holding back, and is undone by the reader rather than by the client** — and the entire critique panel already sits behind a *View / add critiques* click, so *everything visible without interaction* was never the invariant that harness protected. **AND IT FOLLOWS THAT THE FOLD MUST APPLY TO A NEWCOMER TOO:** exempting them would make the artifact render differently per viewer, which is the actual violation. **THE ASSERTION WAS RE-EXPRESSED RATHER THAN RELAXED, and the difference matters because relaxing a gate to fit new code is the failure this file exists to catch.** It now reads: nothing is held back SILENTLY (if anything is folded, a control must name how many), and everything is reachable in one click. That is **strictly more specific than the count it replaced** — a client that dropped a critique, hid one without saying so, or staged one on the basis of being a newcomer all still red, and **it is written against the invariant rather than against this row's threshold**, since an earlier draft pinned it to *four render, one more named* and would have reddened a §4.4 gate the next time 6.22's number moved. **Injected a SILENT fold — the control built and never appended — and it goes 2 red.** | ~~M~~ **BUILT 2026-11-12** | ~~nothing — but sequence it after 6.19~~ **nothing — closed, and gated by `reading-a-thread.mjs` AND `onboarding-ui.mjs` in `ui.yml`** |
| 6.23 | **A byline that is DISTINCT is not a byline that is DISTINGUISHABLE: two critiques by one person and two by two people look the same, because the only identity on screen is twelve base64 characters.** *Opened 2026-11-12 by the pilot's first publisher, on a build that already has 6.18's byline in it: "People writing critiques, still unclear if it is one person or the same. Just text. If 5 people would enter, you wouldnt know if you're speaking to the same or others."* 6.18 read that as *there is no byline* — which was true, and which is now fixed and shipped in v0.3.4 — and the half it did not answer is **the second sentence**, which is not about presence at all. It is about whether a human can tell two of them apart. `short()` renders `${b64(key).slice(0, 12)}…`, and **a 12-character base64 prefix is a string a person compares character by character or not at all.** They are reading the one on screen and correctly reporting that it does not answer their question. **AND THIS ROW'S OWN GATE PASSES WHILE THE DEFECT STANDS, WHICH IS WHY IT IS WORTH WRITING DOWN AS A LESSON RATHER THAN A TASK.** `reading-a-thread.mjs` asserts `distinct.size === 2` over three rows and calls that a control against a byline that is really a constant. It is a good check and it is not the user's question: **distinctness is a property of the strings, legibility is a property of the reader.** A screen can pass every structural assertion in that file and still leave somebody unable to answer *is this the same person as above?* — the gap between *the data is on screen* and *the screen answers the question* is exactly where the pilot keeps finding work, and it is not reachable by any assertion about the DOM. **WHAT IS NOT THE ANSWER.** A display name is not: nothing in the protocol binds one to a key, so a client that showed one would be asserting an identity the DHT cannot vouch for — and under a protocol whose whole premise is that a key answers for what it publishes, an unverifiable label beside a verified key is worse than no label. A count per agent is not: that is the Invariant 1 line the By Author tab already declines to cross. **WHAT MIGHT BE.** Anything DERIVED DETERMINISTICALLY FROM THE KEY ITSELF, so it asserts nothing the key does not already say and every client computes the same thing from the same bytes: a colour, a shape, a two-word mnemonic, an identicon. The property being bought is *same-or-different at a glance*, not *who*. **AND A SECOND, CHEAPER HALF THAT IS INDEPENDENT OF THAT DECISION:** within one panel, *same author as the critique above* is a statement about rows a reader is already looking at, needs no new vocabulary, and is the same species of marker as 6.18's *the claim's author* and 6.19's *the critique's author* — which they have now seen and reacted well to. **THE `[D]` IS WHICH, and it is not obviously ours to settle alone given who reported it twice.** **THE `[M]` IS BUILT 2026-11-13 — and the `[D]` is untouched and still the human's.** A critique whose author already objected on this claim now says *same author as an earlier critique*, beside 6.18's byline and in the same relation line as *you* and *the claim's author*. **IT SAYS "AN EARLIER ONE", NOT "THE ONE ABOVE", and that is the design rather than the wording.** The repeat is frequently not adjacent — this project's own fixture publishes stranger, author, stranger — and an adjacent-only marker would answer the question exactly when it was already easy to answer by eye. **Injected the adjacent-only version: 1 red, and it is the check that carries their sentence.** **IT COUNTS NOTHING.** *"Also wrote 3 of these"* would be a per-agent tally inside one panel, which is the Invariant #1 line the By Author tab already declines to cross; this is an identity statement about two rows the reader is looking at, and nothing is ordered, ranked or totalled by it. **Injected a version that marks every row: 2 red**, both controls. **IT IS DERIVED FROM `list`, NOT FROM 6.22's `shown`, AND THE REASON IS NOT THE OBVIOUS ONE — worth stating because the obvious one is false.** Today the two give identical answers, necessarily: `shown` is a LEADING slice and this search only looks BACKWARD, so for any visible row its author's first appearance is inside the slice too. The equivalence is a property of the fold being a PREFIX. one agent's output without its method rebuilds the canonical measure where no

critique that exists**. A 6.1 screen built today can render exactly one state —
*no cone recorded* — for every critique, which is what `critiques_unseen_by`'s
own note already says. **The feature has no true positives available to it.**

**Item 22's reopen condition is structurally gated on 6.2**, which is a useful
property rather than a coincidence. Both halves are required — *a surface for
readings exists* AND *somebody wants them faster than a person will click* —
and the first is false while 6.2 is unbuilt, so the autonomous-writer question
cannot become live by any other route.

(flag)`, **and the value reached the generated `conductor-config.yaml`** rather than only the JSON; (2) **relaunched with NO flag at all** — `bootstrap_url: ... (stored)`, with no re-write line, which is the single behaviour this row exists for; (3) `--reset-network-config` logged `Network override cleared; using the URLs built into this build.`, deleted the file and fell back to `dev-test-bootstrap2.holochain.org (built-in)`. | 7.16 | **The deployment decision SPEC says must be made DELIBERATELY, and it has two halves rather than one.** *Opened 2026-10-26. Erasure appears in `SECURITY.md`, `INSTALL.md`, `SPEC.md` twice and `READINESS.md`, and in this file ZERO times until now — the same shape as 7.14, with higher stakes.* **SPEC §7's own words:** *"a request to erase personal data — a right under GDPR Article 17 and comparable regimes — cannot be complied with on this network, by anyone, by any means short of every peer abandoning the DNA. That is a deliberate property of the design and not a defect, but it is a decision to make explicitly rather than discover: a private group among informed participants is a different proposition from a public deployment."* **HALF ONE, WHICH IS WRITTEN DOWN: private informed group, or public deployment?** Four invited people who understand it is one proposition; 54 likes on a tweet is another, and almost certainly includes people in the EU/UK, which is what engages Article 3 at all. **HALF TWO, WHICH IS NOT WRITTEN DOWN ANYWHERE: should the default write path put personal data on an immutable store at all?** The accepted mitigation for immutable systems is to keep personal data OFF it and commit only a hash — **and this protocol already has that mechanism, built for a different reason**: `SealedPrediction` is its one private entry type, and §5's hash-then-reveal is exactly the pattern (it was described to Luck Light on 2026-10-26 as *"publish only the hash and reveal later"*). But `create_claim` puts content straight onto the DHT, so **the compliant shape is available and is not the default.** Changing that is a design decision, not a documentation one. **THREE THINGS THAT MAKE THIS SHARPER THAN THE DOCUMENTS CURRENTLY SAY.** (1) **Pseudonymous is still personal**: Recital 26 treats pseudonymised data as personal where re-identification is possible, and an agent key is re-identifiable to anyone who knows whose it is. (2) **Every peer stores other peers' data, so INVITEES MAY INHERIT OBLIGATIONS, not just risk** — the regulatory direction on distributed ledgers (CNIL's 2018 guidance is the usual reference) is that those who decide to write are controllers and those who store may be joint controllers. **`docs/the-invitation.md` discloses permanence and says nothing about this**, which is arguably the larger gap, since permanence affects only their data while controllership affects their duties. (3) **Disclosure and consent do NOT discharge it**: consent is withdrawable, withdrawal triggers an erasure obligation that cannot be performed, and Article 25's data-protection-by-design means building it this way is itself what gets assessed — *"technically impossible"* is not a defence. 7.1 committed to honesty, which this is; it did not and could not commit to immunity. **WHAT REDUCES THE EXPOSURE MOST IS NOT ARCHITECTURAL: keep the content about the world rather than about people.** A network of claims about choline in eggs carries far less than one where people write about each other, and that is a cohort-and-content choice rather than a code change. **AND BEFORE ANY PUBLIC POST OR FUNDING CONVERSATION THIS NEEDS AN HOUR OF ACTUAL LEGAL ADVICE** — not as a hedge: Holo Ventures and comparable funders will ask, and *"erasure is structurally impossible"* is a diligence finding that needs a prepared answer rather than a shrug. **This is a going-wide gate alongside the bump, 7.14 and 7.2's absent audit — and it is the only one of the four this repository already told itself to decide and then did not track.** **ADVICE RECEIVED THE SAME NIGHT, AND IT MOVED ONE HALF AND NOT THE OTHER — `docs/erasure-and-the-content-split.md`.** Informed guidance rather than counsel, recorded with its own hedges intact, and it does NOT discharge 7.2 or replace counsel before a funding conversation. **What it discharges is the question this row was stuck on: whether the architecture has to change BEFORE the invitation. It does not.** (1) **Australia**: the Privacy Act's small-business exemption — most operators at or under **$3M** turnover — still stands as of mid-October 2026 (blogs saying 2026 abolished it are wrong; the OAIC wants it gone and a reform round is being prepared, so it is a fact with a shelf life). **The exposure that survives the exemption is the statutory tort for serious invasions of privacy**, which any individual can sue on — so the real scenario is not the Act, it is somebody writing something genuinely harmful about a person into a store nothing can be removed from. (2) **EU**: Article 3 reaches on paper; enforcement against an Australian individual with no EU presence, revenue or users is very unlikely, **so the variable is 7.13's cohort rather than the code**, and a public post recruiting one EU user is what spends it. (3) **The regulators' preferred shape is the one this protocol already implements once**: EDPB *Guidelines 02/2025* — personal data off-chain, only hashes or proofs on-chain, a proof of existence over identifying content, and a DPIA first where risk is high. **HALF ONE IS THEREFORE UNCHANGED IN DIRECTION** (private informed group; keep the pilot out of the EU) **and HALF TWO IS FILED AS 4.6 RATHER THAN BUILT**, because the advice's second pass withdrew its first: *"no longer a must-do before the invitation, but I'd still note it as a design decision."* **7.4 IS NOT HELD ON THIS ROW.** What the advice asks for before people arrive is in **7.17** and **7.18**, neither of which touches the zomes. **AND THE SALTED-FINGERPRINT ITEM IT FLAGGED AS "do this today" IS ALREADY DONE**: `seal_prediction` rejects an empty salt at `dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs:7249` with the brute-force rationale in the error string, caller-supplied so it is in their hands at reveal time — the rule to carry forward is that any withdrawable-content path inherits it. **One independent confirmation worth keeping**: the advice's *do not build the identity-verification layer* is 3.7's candidate (4), reached here from the invariants rather than the regulations. Two arguments, one conclusion, neither needed the other. | D, and the outside input it asked for is now on file; counsel still wanted before a public post or funding conversation | nothing — and it no longer blocks the invitation |
| 7.17 | ~~**The joining promise and a complaints channel — what 7.16's advice actually asks for before people arrive, and neither needs code.** *Opened 2026-10-26 night.* **THE PROMISE**: invitees affirm that they are responsible for their own content, that posts are permanent, and that **they will not post personal information about anyone else**. As a membrane **required promise** it is signed and ordered, so the agreement itself is on the record — and it is the direct answer to the thing the advice says the pilot needs instead of an architecture change. **It also decides `docs/the-invitation.md` §3**, which is currently open on whether invitees are asked for a `Constitution` at all: this is a concrete thing to ask for, and 7.1 declined *"invite as a pure experiment"* precisely because asking for nothing *"gets testers rather than users"*. **THE LIMIT, STATED SO IT IS NOT OVERSOLD: the people most likely to cause trouble never signed it.** A third party written about by a member is not bound by the members' promises; an indemnity from a pseudonymous key is worth what you can collect from a key; and statutory protections generally cannot be signed away. **Exposure also tracks ROLE rather than text** — writing software others run is far from liability, while running the bootstrap (7.14), founding the membranes and inviting the users is closer to operating, which is the current position. **THE COMPLAINTS CHANNEL IS THE OTHER HALF, AND THE MECHANISMS ARE ALREADY BUILT**: a `Retraction` by the author, an `AntibodyPattern` by anyone (SPEC §7's additive remedies), plus removal from a reader's own trust list and root set (3.7). What is missing is a **stated** channel and a promise to act on it, so that *"nothing can be removed"* is not the same sentence as *"nothing was done"*. **The defamation-reform reason behind this is a question rather than a conclusion** — intermediary protections generally assume a complaints process and some available act, and how that applies to a decentralised network, or in SA specifically, was explicitly not settled by the advice. **WRITTEN 2026-10-26 NIGHT — `docs/the-joining-promise.md`, 165 lines.** The promise is three phrases in the idiom the harnesses already use, published by the invitee as a `Constitution` — *answer for what I publish*, *treat everything I publish as permanent*, *publish no personal information about another person* — with a fourth offered because the complaints channel needs it (*answer a complaint about something I published*: a promise to RESPOND, not to remove). treat everything I publish as permanent; publish no personal information about another person; answer a complaint about something I published.* The fourth is in deliberately, because the complaints channel needs somebody who has promised to RESPOND — never to remove, since removal does not exist. **Both halves are now in `docs/the-invitation.md`**: §3 carries the ask, the exact payloads (via `docs/the-joining-promise.md` §1) and the order the protocol half-enforces — the founder publishes their own four first, because `create_membrane` refuses a membrane whose constitution is not the founder's own and refuses an empty `required_promises`, both DHT-side — and **§1 now carries the ask and the complaints channel in the invitee's own language**, including the sentence that matters: *nothing can be deleted* and *nothing will be done* are different sentences. **The limits are stated in §3 rather than left implicit**: it binds nobody who never joined, cannot be enforced against a pseudonymous key, and some protections cannot be signed away — which is 4.6's and 3.9's territory, both filed and neither built. | ~~M to write the text~~ **WRITTEN**; The flag is advisory, not blocking — confirmed by the author in the submission flow — so withdrawing was judged not worth resetting a live project page.* **THE FALSE CLAIM, WHICH MATTERS MORE THAN THE FLAG:** the goals section says *"A critique type can require evidence before it will accept one."* `required_evidence` is a declared `Vec<String>` on `CritiqueSpecies` (`dna/integrity/src/lib.rs:384`) and **that declaration is its only occurrence in the entire DNA** — no validation rule and no coordinator function reads it. Nothing refuses a critique for lacking the evidence its species declares. **This is 1.12 exactly** — *three sources disagree about whether `min_attestations` is enforced, and the read PUBLISHES the lie* — and it is the second time this repository has shipped a declared-but-unenforced field as an enforced one in outward-facing text. ~~**THE REPOSITORY IS INNOCENT AND THAT NARROWS THE FIX:**~~ **THAT WAS WRONG, AND FINDING IT OUT IS WHY THE ROW WAS RE-VERIFIED RATHER THAN TRUSTED — CORRECTED AND FIXED 2026-10-30.** `README.md:455` and `SPEC.md:139` are indeed innocent: the first lists the field and claims nothing of it, the second lists it with an **empty validation column**. **THE SHIPPED UI WAS NOT.** `mobile-ui/src/main.ts` asserted the gate **twice**, to every user of the desktop app: the species card's label read ***"A critique of this type must cite:"***, and the proposal form's placeholder read ***"Evidence a critique of this type must cite (one per line, optional)"*** — **which contradicts itself inside one sentence**, promising a requirement and then calling it optional. So the overstatement was not only in the funding drafts where no reader could check it; it was **on screen, in the product, above the field itself**. **THAT MAKES THE UI A PREREQUISITE FOR THE CORRECTION RATHER THAN A SEPARATE TASK.** A public comment saying *nothing enforces `required_evidence`*, posted while the shipped app tells its users a critique of that type **must** cite, is a correction the product immediately falsifies — and this row exists because an unfalsifiable-looking claim went out. **FIXED:** the label now reads *"This type asks a critique to cite:"*, the placeholder matches, and a `required-evidence` concept note states the limit **where the list is rendered** — *"NOTHING ENFORCES IT: the protocol accepts a critique of this type that cites none of it, and no rule reads this list"* — which is 6.1's honesty rule applied to a different unenforced field, and the idiom `friction-budget` (*"Nothing buys past it"*) and `discourse-health` (*"nothing acts on it"*) already use. `tsc --noEmit` clean. **AND IT IS 1.12 A THIRD TIME, NOT A SECOND** — declared-but-unenforced rendered as enforced, now found in the DNA's own client. **A SMALLER ADJACENT ONE IS LEFT FOR THE AUTHOR TO JUDGE, NOT TOUCHED HERE:** the `required-promises` concept note (`onboarding.ts:156`) calls a membrane's promises *"the entry condition"*, while **6.6 records that joining is unrestricted by deliberate decision** — *"a protocol that refused the join would make the membrane an authority over who may speak."* *"Entry condition"* reads as a gate; the note's *"accountability instead of a fee"* half gestures at the truth. That is 6.6's territory and a wording call rather than a false enforcement claim, so it is flagged rather than changed. The *other* half of the sentence is true, enforced and gated: critiques are rate-limited at 20/hour, guarded by `scripts/live-verify/friction-limits.mjs`. **WHAT IS OWED: ONE COMMENT ON THE PROJECT PAGE COVERING BOTH** — the AI-assistance disclosure and this correction. Both in the author's own words, for the reason in 7.24. A project arguing that undisclosed denominators corrupt a record, publishing its own correction unprompted within a day, is the thesis demonstrated rather than asserted; the same text laundered past a detector is not. | **H** — his words, and the disclosure is void in anyone else's | nothing |
| 7.26 | ~~**Four findings in Your Own AI's documented build, drafted and NOT FILED.**~~ **FILED 2026-10-30 as `WeAreFlowsta/Your-Own-AI` issue #3** — open, authored by `StateofIntent`, body 8,695 chars, `docs/upstream/your-own-ai-build-from-source.md` posted unedited, and the covering X DM sent the same day (`docs/funding/eric-doriean-letter.md`, local-only). **NOTHING ON THIS SIDE IS OUTSTANDING.** The one thing that could reopen it is his answer: the DM offered a PR instead of an issue, since three of the four are one-line diffs, and if he takes that up it is new work rather than this row. **ONE SELF-CORRECTION WAS POSTED 2026-10-31 02:44Z — [issue #3 comment 6537956104](https://github.com/WeAreFlowsta/Your-Own-AI/issues/3#issuecomment-6537956104), 2,096 characters; the ADDENDUM at the foot of `docs/upstream/your-own-ai-build-from-source.md` carries it.** His `2089e61` (pinned SHA-256s for the sidecars) landed ~11 hours BEFORE the issue was filed and showed that `build-release.yml` downloads `holochain` and `lair-keystore` straight from `holochain/holochain` releases — *"nothing compiled from source"*, in its own comment — so **finding 2's recommended fix (four `cargo install` commands) was worse than the path his repo already contains**, and the defect is better described as `download-holochain-binaries.sh` being STALE relative to the workflow than as a missing release. The other three findings were re-checked against current `origin/HEAD` and all hold. It was posted before he had replied, deliberately: the uncorrected report sends a reader to compile the conductor from crates.io, so every hour it stood alone risked wasting his time, while the cost of being early is one notification. 7.27's webkit note stays held by contrast, because it ASKS him to pick a tracker rather than removing a demand. The findings text below stays as the durable record of what was measured. *Found 2026-10-29 while building it from source as 6.10's local-inference route; drafted `docs/upstream/your-own-ai-build-from-source.md` 2026-10-30, every claim re-verified that day rather than taken from the session's notes — which corrected two of them.* README:118 gives the build as five commands and **three of the four fail outside the org.** **(1)** `download-llama-binaries.sh:16` sets `REPO="Your-Own-AI/App"`, a repository that 404s, while the sibling script carries the same comment and the right value — and because the tag now comes from `engine.rs` by `sed` rather than from the API, and the download is `curl -L` without `-f`, it writes **four 9-byte files containing `Not Found`**, `chmod +x`s them, prints four green ticks and exits 0. The silence is the severity: the first symptom is a Tauri sidecar that is a text file. All four assets are public at `WeAreFlowsta/Your-Own-AI` tag `llama-b11669` — `HTTP 200` on each, checked 2026-10-30 — so the fix is one line plus `-f`. **(2)** `download-holochain-binaries.sh:15` has the RIGHT repo and still cannot succeed: there is no `holochain-*` tag in the first 100 releases, and the workflow its error message tells you to run (`build-holochain-binaries.yml`) **is not in the repository**, so neither of its two suggestions is followable even in a fork. The draft supplies the four `cargo install` commands that do work, with `--root` in the instruction rather than beside it. **(3)** that script's final `ls` globs `holochain-*` and `lair-keystore-*` while every download is written with the `yourowai-` prefix `tauri.conf.json:113-114` requires, so under its own `set -e` a fully successful run prints `✅ All Holochain binaries downloaded!` and then exits 2. **(4)** the Node version is pinned in CI (`build-release.yml:131`, `node-version: 20`) and nowhere a local build can see it — no `engines`, no `.nvmrc`, README opening on a bare `npm install`; Node 26 fails building `sharp` through `@builder.io/qwik-city` → `vite-imagetools`. **WHAT RE-VERIFICATION CORRECTED, and it is the 7.25 lesson applied before publishing rather than after:** the session's notes said *both* download scripts used the dead repo (only one does — the other fails for an entirely different reason) and that the Linux CI ran on an older image for the *llama* build (it is the *release* build, `build-release.yml:95`). Both would have been wrong in somebody else's tracker. **The draft also states what it does not claim:** the app builds clean and its whole backend works — lair, conductor, happ, agent key, `llama-server` bound — and the webview crash is 7.27's and is not theirs | ~~X~~ **DONE** | **Filed by the human, on the human's account, 2026-10-30 — issue #3.** The model drafted and verified; it did not file, and the DM went in the human's own words from the local-only draft |
| 7.27 | ~~**The WebKitGTK 2.52.6 webview crash — drafted as a COMPATIBILITY NOTE, OFFERED 2026-10-30, and still not filed.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-10-31 — HE REPLIED, AND HE HAD ALREADY FOUND IT.** Holding this row for his choice of tracker was the right call and it cost nothing: the venue question answered itself. The cause is a **WebKitGTK 2.52 bug that fires when NVIDIA's EGL does the drawing** — Flowsta reproduced it in-house on a machine drawing through NVIDIA, a second Linux user reported it the same week, and WebKit's own MiniBrowser crashes in the same frames on a plain page mutating the DOM every 400 ms, while machines drawing through Mesa did not crash in any of their tests. **Flowsta reports it to WebKit themselves**, so neither tracker was ever ours, and nothing on this side is outstanding. Their list of switches that did NOT help (DMABUF off, forced SHM, compositing off, Skia CPU, `LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE`, GBM off) matches the draft's own. **The workaround is one variable** — `__EGL_VENDOR_LIBRARY_FILENAMES=/usr/share/glvnd/egl_vendor.d/50_mesa.json` — and Flowsta will set it at startup when the NVIDIA driver is loaded, across all three of their Tauri apps. **VERIFIED HERE 2026-10-31, and the GUI ran on this machine for the first time:** same packages as the crash run (`pacman.log` records no upgrades in between, so the variable is the only change), app under native Wayland with its window mapped 927x1056 and the onboarding screen rendering. **Two things the verification added that his report did not have:** (a) the Error 71 reproduces on a **static page with no JS**, so DOM mutation was never required to trigger it and **Wayland was incidental — NVIDIA's EGL is the trigger**; (b) **XWayland is NOT an escape from the bug — a claim this row made and then corrected.** Under `GDK_BACKEND=x11` with NVIDIA's EGL the process survives, which is what *survived 40s* originally recorded, but the window **maps blank and no `WebKitWebProcess` ever starts** — measured properly 2026-10-31 after the first pass checked only that the process had not exited. The backend comes up fully behind the empty window, which is what makes it misread as working. The Mesa variable remains the only configuration here where the UI appears. (c) on this card Mesa has no driver for the GM204 (`pci id 10de:13c2, driver (null)`, `DRI2: failed to create screen`), so the variable means **software rendering for the webview** — the right trade against a crash, but a trade, and worth him knowing before it is set for every NVIDIA user. **GPU compute is unaffected** (`GPU detected: NVIDIA GeForce GTX 970 - 4.2 GB`), so the cost is rendering and never inference. The downgrade-to-`2.48.6` candidate is **moot**. `docs/upstream/your-own-ai-webkit-2.52-linux.md` now ends in a *Resolution* section carrying the confirmation matrix; it is a record, not a filing. The original draft below stays as the durable account of what was measured before the cause was known. *The X DM accompanying 7.26's issue #3 described the finding in three sentences and put the venue to him explicitly — his tracker, `bugs.webkit.org`, or neither. So this row now waits on HIS answer rather than on a decision here, which is the right place for it: a report saying "your app crashes" that turns out to be a system webkit bug is the one move that would cost credibility, and letting the maintainer pick the tracker removes that risk entirely.* *Drafted `docs/upstream/your-own-ai-webkit-2.52-linux.md` 2026-10-30; the isolating test re-run that day.* **IT IS NOT THEIR BUG AND THE DRAFT SAYS SO IN ITS FIRST LINE** — the whole reason it needs a decision rather than a send. Your Own AI's backend comes up fully and the web process then SIGSEGVs ~1s after the UI mounts, taking the app with it (`WebKitWebProcess` dies first, `app` a second later, every launch; `SEGV_MAPERR`, frame 11 in anonymous executable memory reached through `JSC::profiledCall`, which is the shape of a crash in generated code and is NOT asserted as the mechanism — `JSC_useJIT=0` did not prevent it and Arch ships no webkit debug symbols). **The isolating test is the finding:** WebKitGTK's own `MiniBrowser about:blank`, no Tauri anywhere, dies in ~1s with `Gdk-Message: Error 71 (Protocol error) dispatching to Wayland display`, and under `GDK_BACKEND=x11` survives 20s while logging `Failed to create GBM buffer of size 2048x1460` against the NVIDIA stack. So: `webkit2gtk-4.1 2.52.6-1` + Hyprland 0.56.2 Wayland + nvidia 580.178.04 on a GTX 970, and the app's crash and MiniBrowser's protocol error are two exits from one root. **WHY IT GOES TO THEM AT ALL:** Tauri links the system webkit, so their official `.deb` fails identically on any distro at 2.52 and no packaging change avoids it; their CI builds Linux on `ubuntu-22.04` (`build-release.yml:95`) and never launches a webview, so the combination is untested by construction; and from the user's side the symptom is indistinguishable from their bug — window appears, vanishes, logs end mid-success. `scripts/check-webview-floor.mjs` shows they already treat the OLD end of the WebKitGTK range as a real constraint, and this is the same constraint from the new end. **The ask is only a known-issue line plus a startup log line recording the resolved webkit version**, which is the one thing a diagnostics file should carry for a crash this fast — and README:136 already asks reporters to attach one. The untried candidate, downgrading to `2.48.6-1` with `IgnorePkg`, is in the draft as **not attempted and not recommended**: four minor versions of browser-engine security fixes for a GUI, unconfirmed as a fix **AND IT IS SHIPPED, 2026-11-01.** He replied again: the fix is built and **ships in the next Your Own AI release** — when the NVIDIA driver is loaded and the user has set neither variable themselves, the app uses Mesa's EGL with WebKit's GBM path off, with `FLOWSTA_WEBVIEW_FIX=0` to turn it off and a plan to drop it once WebKitGTK is fixed. **The llvmpipe cost was already known to them and chosen on purpose** — a working window beats a crash or a blank one, and the engine stays on the GPU — so the "worth them knowing it is the trade" note was not news. **What they took is the static-`h1` reproduction from this side**, cleaner than their own DOM-mutating one because it needs no JavaScript at all; they will find or file the WebKit report themselves. **Nothing further is owed and nothing is filed from here.** | ~~X~~ **DONE — nothing to file** | **Resolved upstream by the maintainer, not by a filing from here.** The 2026-10-30 DM put the venue to him; his 2026-10-31 reply carried the cause, the workaround and the plan to report it to WebKit. The `bugs.webkit.org` section of the draft is therefore spent |
| 7.28 | ~~**`partition-rejoin` is red on every pull request**~~ **— IT IS NOT, CORRECTED 2026-10-31 BY THE NEXT RUN — ~~and the 205.5s is the first figure this phenomenon has produced that fits nothing already documented.~~ **— ALSO FALSE, CORRECTED 2026-10-31 AGAINST `READINESS.md`: 205.5s is one 5-second poll from the 210.5s already on the record, and this row had been reading the README's six-figure summary instead of READINESS's nine-figure list.** *Opened 2026-10-30 after the check failed on PR #391, a branch that cannot have caused it.* **WHAT IS FAILING IS A PRECONDITION, NOT AN ASSERTION, and the distinction is the whole row.** `partition-rejoin.mjs:760` checks that the Phase 0 baseline — one claim crossing nodeA → nodeB with **both nodes up and nothing partitioned** — lands inside `MAX_SOUND_BASELINE_MS`. On both recent runs every substantive assertion PASSED: the partition held, nodeB returned and converged in **5.0s**, the isolated nodeC saw nothing throughout, and both nodes answered at the end. So a red `partition-rejoin` here is **not** a protocol regression and must not be read as one. **THE THREE NEW OCCURRENCES, AND THE THIRD ONE PASSED.** `425.8s` on `main` 2026-10-28 (run 40704216520, the #390 release merge, job 14m46s); `205.5s` on PR #391 2026-10-30 (run 41042209076, job 11m49s); then **`10.0s` — PASS, `ALL CHECKS PASSED` — on the SAME branch about twenty minutes later** (run 41044792444, job 4m26s, poll 3 of the 5.0s sampler), which is the run #391 merged on. **THAT THIRD FIGURE IS THE MOST USEFUL THING IN THIS ROW** and it was not available when the row was written: a healthy baseline minutes from a 205.5s one, on code that differs by one documentation commit, is evidence for environment-dependent intermittency rather than for anything deterministic about THIS pair of runs. It also means the harness is not broken and needs no repair to keep being trusted. ~~**425.8s IS NOT NEWS** … **205.5s IS DIFFERENT, and it is the only reason this row exists** … close to **half** of ~420s, which under the existing reading would be the same schedule caught at a different phase.~~ **ALL OF THAT IS STRUCK, 2026-10-31, AND IT WAS WRONG TWICE OVER.** **(i) THE RECORD IS `READINESS.md:735`, NOT `README:1266`.** The authoritative list is nine figures — 175.4s, **210.5s**, 415s, 421.1s, 424.9s, 884.8s (×2, local three-node), 929.1s, 943.0s — and **210.5s has been on it since #278**, recorded there as an occurrence *on a change that provably could not have caused it*, which is exactly 205.5s's position on #391. One 5-second poll separates the two figures, and `awaitConvergence` cannot distinguish crossings inside one sampling interval. So 205.5s is an ordinary member of a documented distribution and **does not clear §9's reopen condition.** **(ii) THE READING IT LEANED ON IS RETRACTED IN PLACE.** `READINESS.md:764` strikes the deterministic-schedule hypothesis and demonstrates why: identical reported figures come from variance **smaller** than the 5s sampling interval, by construction — true crossings at 418.0s, 419.5s, 420.9s and 421.0s all report as `421.1s`. The "half of ~420s" argument therefore rested on a superseded claim, reached by citing the README paragraph that still carries the old version instead of the correction. **THE MECHANISM OF BOTH ERRORS IS THE SAME:** this row consulted one file and not its sibling — [[sweep-adjacent-claims-not-just-the-one-you-came-for]], committed inside a row whose other evidence was checked line by line, and surfaced only because two branches were being audited for something else entirely. **SO THE LIVE DECISION IS A DIFFERENT ONE, AND IT IS NOT §9's.** §9's freeze stays shut: these are *"another slow crossing"*, which its reopen condition names as insufficient. What has moved is the **retry's own retirement condition**. `READINESS.md:521` left the count at *"nine observations, five of them slow enough to fail without a retry… five of nine is not yet 'most', and the next occurrence decides whether it becomes so."* These two are the next occurrences. **The count is now ELEVEN, and the slow tally is six or seven depending on a classification this row cannot make**: 425.8s exceeds both harnesses' windows and is unambiguously slow, while 205.5s is UNDER `real-gossip`'s 330s window and OVER `partition-rejoin`'s 60s precondition — the same split the ninth observation recorded as *"simultaneously a pass-with-a-warning in one harness and a failure in the other"*. **Six of eleven is already "most"**, and `real-gossip.mjs`'s `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS` comment hangs its own falsification on that word: *"a retry needed on most runs is not an intermittency being absorbed, it is a routine failure being hidden."* So the question for a person is whether the retry is now hiding a routine failure — with the **10.0s** crossing minutes after the 205.5s standing as the live evidence that the intermittency premise has not simply become false. All three occurrences are recorded at `READINESS.md:530` where the count lives, rather than only here, because recording them only in this row would repeat the error above. **WHAT THIS ROW IS NOT ASKING FOR: raising the 60s.** `MAX_SOUND_BASELINE_MS` is DERIVED — `DWELL_CAP_MS / 5` at `partition-rejoin.mjs:280` — because the dwell before asserting absence must outlast gossip by 5x or the absence proves nothing. Raising the threshold without raising the 300s cap makes the divergence assertion weaker than designed while printing a clean PASS, and the comment at `:786-796` records that the first version of this fix did precisely that and was reverted: *"a label claiming more than its assertion tested — this directory's named failure mode."* Raising the cap instead costs job budget against a 600s convergence window. **The harness's own instruction is the default action:** *"one occurrence is not a distribution, so record it beside the others before changing any window."* This row IS that recording. ~~**THE ACTUAL NEW COST, which is not latency.** Every PR is now red on this check, so a red `partition-rejoin` has stopped carrying information — the next real failure in that harness arrives indistinguishable from this one. That is a signal-quality problem with a deadline attached, and it is the half of this row that gets worse by waiting.~~ **STRUCK 2026-10-31: BOTH SENTENCES WERE FALSE, and the next run disproved them before this row was a day old.** `partition-rejoin` passed at 10.0s on the following run, so it is not red on every pull request, a red result still carries information, and nothing here degrades on a clock. The error was generalising a persistent failure from two consecutive ones — which is [[sweep-adjacent-claims-not-just-the-one-you-came-for]]'s defect in its other direction: not an unswept sibling, but a claim extrapolated past the two data points that actually supported it, written into the same row whose evidence had been checked carefully. **NOTHING IN THIS ROW IS NOW URGENT.** The 205.5s is still the first figure in neither documented cluster, so §9's reopen question stands exactly as stated below — it simply stands without a deadline. **NOT TO BE CONFLATED WITH 7.8**, which is the returning-node asymmetry: that one is a known peer recovering slower than a stranger, measured across the public internet. This is a FIRST crossing between two conductors on one GitHub runner with nothing offline. Both plausibly live in `kitsune2` scheduling, and that is as far as the shared claim goes. **THREE MORE OBSERVATIONS 2026-11-01, AND THE COUNT NOW ANSWERS THE OPEN CALL ARITHMETICALLY.** The eight negative-evidence PRs (#407, #410-#415) are **documentation-only** — comments in `scripts/live-verify/*.mjs` and one README table row each, no code path into any harness — and `partition-rejoin`'s `<= 60s` baseline precondition failed on three of them: **95.3s** (#407), **421.1s** (#411), **421.1s** (#413). So the count goes from eleven to **fourteen, nine-or-ten slow** depending on how 205.5s still classifies. **95.3s is a NEW VALUE in the distribution** — every prior figure sat either near the 5s floor or above 200s, and a baseline that is slow but not *minutes*-slow is the first evidence against a purely bimodal reading. **AND THAT ARITHMETIC WAS WRONG — WITHDRAWN 2026-11-02, THE SAME PASS THAT WROTE IT.** It said *nine of fourteen is past "most runs" on any reading* and offered the **D** as satisfied. **Fourteen is the count of SLOW-CROSSING OBSERVATIONS, not of RUNS**, so dividing slow by slow answers nothing; *most runs* needs every run in the denominator. Corrected: of the fourteen observations, **ten exceed `real-gossip`'s 330s window** and therefore would need a retry (415s, 421.1s, 424.9s, 425.8s, 884.8s ×2, 929.1s, 943.0s, and 2026-11-01's two 421.1s), while **four are slow but UNDER 330s and need none** (175.4s, 205.5s, 210.5s, and 95.3s). Against **100+ `network.yml` runs** that is a retry on roughly **10% of runs — not most.** **The new 95.3s figure made the case WEAKER, not stronger**, being under 330s. **A SECOND CONFLATION in the withdrawn version:** `partition-rejoin`'s `<= 60s` precondition failures were counted as evidence about `real-gossip`'s `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS`. Those are two thresholds on one phenomenon and only the >330s subset bears on the retry. **SO THE RETIREMENT CONDITION IS NOT MET and the D is NOT satisfied** — `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS` stays at 3. Retiring it here would redden CI on ~10% of runs for a documented, understood intermittency, which is the flaky-red-meaning-nothing this repository refuses, and `JOB_CEILING_MS`'s own note adds that the retries and the job timeout cannot be chosen independently. **AND THAT MEASUREMENT IS NOW TAKEN, 2026-11-02 — THE CONDITION IS ANSWERED NO AND THE D IS CLOSED.** Method: `real-gossip.mjs:1206` emits `::warning::THE CROSSING NEEDED n ATTEMPTS` exactly when `crossing.n > 1`, so every retry is in the logs by construction. Scanned the 59 `network.yml` runs GitHub still holds (2026-10-31 to 2026-11-01), excluding **6 where the crossing was never measured** — a run that died before the crossing is evidence of nothing, and counting it either way is the denominator error this row already withdrew once. **RESULT: 7 of 53 runs needed a retry, 13.2%. *"Most runs"* would require 27 of 53.** So the premise `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS` rests on has NOT changed, it is absorbing an intermittency rather than hiding a routine failure, and **the retry stays.** **AND THE SAME SCAN SHOWS 3 IS LOAD-BEARING RATHER THAN SPARE, which is the finding nobody was looking for:** of the seven, six crossed on the SECOND attempt — but run `41289924229` needed **all three, on both of its job attempts**. `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS: 2` would have reddened that run. The comment above the constant argued for three on burst-limiter grounds (15 initiations per peer per 10 minutes, so two attempts at 330s can land inside one window); **that argument is now observed rather than reasoned.** **TWO LIMITS ON THE FIGURE, stated so it is not over-read.** It is a **FLOOR on retries and a ceiling on clean runs**: `gh run list` reports a run's CURRENT conclusion, so a run that needed a retry and was then re-run clean reads clean — the same instrument problem README §7b.2 records for the `ui` rate, where the honest answer was published as a band for this reason. And the window is **two days of heavy docs-only CI**, which is a biased sample in the direction that would INFLATE the rate (a contended runner is what makes the crossing slow), so 13.2% is if anything generous to the retirement case. **The 205.5s classification no longer gates it** — at nine of fourteen the answer is *most* whether 205.5s counts or not, which is the first time this row has not needed that question settled first. **Do not raise the 60s**: `MAX_SOUND_BASELINE_MS` is `DWELL_CAP_MS / 5`, and raising it alone prints a clean PASS on a weaker assertion — already tried and reverted **A FIFTEENTH OBSERVATION, 2026-11-08 — and it is recorded rather than retried.** Main's post-merge run at `cd1f25d` (PR #439, row 1.25's build) failed `partition-rejoin`'s Phase 0 precondition — *the baseline is fast enough to measure divergence inside the job budget (`<= 60s`)* — with the baseline crossing **421.2s** on an UNPARTITIONED network, both nodes up. The `network` harness job in the same run PASSED and the only other non-pass was this harness's own deliberate INCONCLUSIVE, which the log points away from in as many words: *"See the baseline check above for the thing that is actually wrong."* The log census shows no zome-level errors, and the diff was coordinator-only — **another observation on a diff incapable of causing it**, which is this row's oldest pattern. **IT COUNTS IN THE >330s SUBSET BY ITS FIGURE, NOT BY ITS THRESHOLD, and the distinction is the one this row already had to make once:** what was wrong in the withdrawn version was counting `<= 60s` PRECONDITION FAILURES as evidence about `real-gossip`'s `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS`; a measured crossing of 421.2s is a >330s crossing whichever harness observed it. So the tally is **fifteen observations, eleven over 330s** (415s, 421.1s ×3, 421.2s, 424.9s, 425.8s, 884.8s ×2, 929.1s, 943.0s) and **four under** (95.3s, 175.4s, 205.5s, 210.5s). **THE RETIREMENT ARITHMETIC IS UNCHANGED IN DIRECTION:** eleven against a `network.yml` run count that has itself grown past 100 is still roughly a tenth, and still not *most runs* — the figure that would retire `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS` is a proportion of RUNS, and slow observations are not its denominator. **NOT RE-RUN FOR A GREEN BADGE, DELIBERATELY.** A retry here would have removed the observation and left the badge, and the observation is what the open call rests on. **AND THE 60s STAYS**: `MAX_SOUND_BASELINE_MS` is `DWELL_CAP_MS / 5`, so raising it alone prints a clean PASS on a weaker assertion — already tried and reverted. **A SIXTEENTH, THE SAME DAY — AND THE PROPORTION IS NOW MEASURED RATHER THAN ARGUED.** PR #444's head (`feat/exclude-self-attestation`, a coordinator-only diff touching the ATTESTATION WALK) failed the same Phase 0 precondition with the baseline crossing **421.0s**, 2026-11-08, about ten hours after the fifteenth. One failing check again, and again on a diff that cannot cause it: the change excludes an agent from their own attester list and cannot make a claim take seven minutes to cross between two nodes on one machine. **TALLY: sixteen observations, twelve over `real-gossip`'s 330s window** (415s, 421.0s, 421.1s ×3, 421.2s, 424.9s, 425.8s, 884.8s ×2, 929.1s, 943.0s) and **four under** (95.3s, 175.4s, 205.5s, 210.5s). **THE FIGURES CLUSTERING AT ~421s ARE NOT SIX INDEPENDENT MAGNITUDES, and this row's own header already says why:** the sampler's resolution is ±5.0s, so *an identical figure across runs is expected from this instrument, not anomalous*. 421.0, 421.1 and 421.2 are the SAME mode recurring, not evidence of a new one — counting them as distinct magnitudes would be reading precision into an instrument that has none. **WHAT IS GENUINELY NEW IS THE DENOMINATOR.** This row's retirement condition is *a retry needed on most RUNS*, and slow observations were never its denominator. 2026-11-08 supplied **four** `partition-rejoin` runs in one day — `cd1f25d` (421.2s, FAIL), `866f1f1` (PASS), PR #444's first head (421.0s, FAIL), and **PR #444's second head (PASS)** — so **two of four**, against roughly a tenth across 100+. **The fourth run is the pattern this row already records: the fifteenth and sixteenth were each followed by a pass on the very next run, exactly as 425.8s and 205.5s were followed by 10.0s.** One day is not a rate, and a day at half against a long-run tenth is the first thing here worth watching deliberately rather than recording passively. **If the coming days hold near half, the retirement condition is met and `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS` goes; if they revert to a tenth, today was a cluster and the long-run figure stands.** Either way the measurement to make is runs-per-day, not more slow crossings. **STILL NOT RE-RUN, AND THE 60s STILL STAYS** — both for the reasons recorded against the fifteenth. | **D** — ~~rule whether 205.5s clears §9's reopen condition~~ **(ANSWERED 2026-10-31: it does not)**; | 7.29 | **The pilot network only exists while two participants happen to have the app open at the same moment, and nothing in the project says so.** *Opened 2026-11-09, by trying and failing to verify a participant's "i published the claims".* **DECIDED AND BUILT THE SAME DAY — see THE NODE THAT EXISTS at the end of this row; what is still open is the DISCLOSURE, not the machine.** **MEASURED, three ways agreeing.** Driving the shipped `v0.3.3` desktop build: `get_claims_by_domain("Mental Health Reform")` returned zero while the control domain `xnet-1026-a` rendered its claim, so the read path was working; `admin.agentInfo` returned **one** record — this machine's own agent, `storageArc: null` — and stayed at one across 3+ minutes of polling; and the bootstrap server itself, asked directly (`GET https://dev-test-bootstrap2.holochain.org/bootstrap/<space>`, where `<space>` is the 32 bytes at offset 3 of the DNA hash, base64url), **lists exactly one agent for this DNA: ours.** **So the zero was not evidence about the participant.** Agent info on the bootstrap server expires within the hour, so somebody who publishes and closes the app is gone from the network before anyone can read them. **WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT THREATEN.** Permanence is untouched — a claim is on its author's own source chain the moment it is committed, and 1.4 proved both directions cross between packaged installs. What is missing is **reachability**, and the pilot's working assumption — *publish when you can, I'll read it later* — depends on exactly that. With two or three laptop participants and no always-on node, the common case is an honest read returning nothing, which is also 6.13's empty state stated as an operational fact rather than a UI one. **AND THE PERSISTENT AGENT DOES NOT COVER FOR IT, FOR A REASON THAT WAS MISDIAGNOSED FIRST AND THEN MEASURED.** `~/.epi-agent/inhabitant` holds `dht-uhC0kb-i4w7LvWd-pzqG3Wmm76P0U4pFZ3yGXhWzp-aRCpinsVDdh.db`; the desktop 0.3.x profile holds `dht-uhC0kozxlYwR0REOn2iS4NswBOPzJ7I6kKsRV6o3WOEuoQOuEi-4o.db`. **The first guess — that the repo's `.happ` had drifted from the release — was wrong, and `git log` says so: no commit has touched `dna/integrity/src/lib.rs` since 2026-10-24.** The real cause is the one this file already records at §0: `uhC0kb-i4w7…` is the BUNDLE's unseeded hash, and the desktop applies a network seed at install — `${productName}-${breakingAppVersion()}`, i.e. the literal `Epistemic Resonance-0.3.x` (`epistemic-resonance-desktop/src/main/cli.ts:102`, `filesystem.ts:190`). **PROVEN RATHER THAN INFERRED:** installing this repo's own `epistemic-resonance-happ.happ` with `network_seed: 'Epistemic Resonance-0.3.x'` yields `uhC0kozxlYwR0REOn2iS4NswBOPzJ7I6kKsRV6o3WOEuoQOuEi-4o` exactly — probe installed, hash read, uninstalled without ever being enabled, so it never joined anything. **THE `[M]` HALF IS ONE LINE:** `stand-up-and-verify.mjs` passes no `network_seed` to `installApp`, and adding that string puts the inhabitant on the pilot DHT with a full arc, which is the only always-on node this project would have. **THE `[D]` HALF IS WHY IT HAS NOT BEEN DONE.** (1) It stands a SECOND key belonging to the same person in a network whose whole premise is that a key answers for what it publishes — the inhabitant already declares `declared_automated_agent` and `automated_key_answered_for_by_named_person` precisely so this is disclosure rather than a sybil, but declaring it to a DHT nobody else is on has never tested the claim on anyone. (2) **It restarts the tenure clock.** Tenure is read off the `AgentToMembrane` join action timestamp, so a join on the pilot DNA begins a new 30 days and the 2026-12-08 date in [[persistent-agent-tenure-clock]] moves — **though that date is worth little as it stands, because the grant it is accruing is on a DHT with one member and can never be exercised by anybody but itself.** (3) An always-on full-arc node holds every claim anyone publishes, which is the deployment question 7.16 already has open, pointed at a machine rather than at a policy. **THE FREE ALTERNATIVE, and it should be said to participants either way:** tell them the network needs overlap — publish and leave it open, and say when. That costs nothing, needs no decision, and is the honest description of what they have. **THE NODE THAT EXISTS, 2026-11-09 — and the one-line fix named above is NOT the fix that was applied.** Editing `stand-up-and-verify.mjs` would have put the DECLARED AUTOMATED AGENT into the pilot, and that is a decision already made the other way: its own record says it is *a declared instrument, not a participant... NOT in the pilot and must not be*, because an AI the author prompts is the author one layer removed, and under Invariant 6 that record could never be tidied up afterwards. Founding `ProtocolInhabitant` on the pilot DNA would have overturned that silently, as a side effect of a logistics fix. **SO THE SPLIT THAT MATTERED WAS INFRASTRUCTURE VERSUS RECORD, NOT WHETHER TO HAVE A NODE.** What was built is a SEPARATE root — `~/.epi-pilot-node`, a fresh key, admin **9499** (clear of `~/.epi-agent`'s 8899/8898, `sandbox.sh`'s 8888/8889 and `swarm.sh`'s 9200+), a **hand-written** conductor config so no `.hc` exists for it and `hc sandbox clean` cannot reach it from any directory, and a **systemd user unit with lingering enabled**, so it survives logout and reboot rather than a terminal session. **IT PUBLISHES NOTHING, AND THAT IS MEASURED ON EVERY RUN RATHER THAN ASSERTED:** `~/.epi-pilot-node/verify.mjs` dumps the node's own chain and prints **3 records, 0 App entries** — genesis only; no constitution, no membrane, no join — and prints `VIOLATED` the moment anything calls a zome function on it. Neither zome has an `init` callback, so enabling the app writes no app entry by construction rather than by restraint. **THE REST, ALSO MEASURED:** the cell's DNA is `uhC0kozxlYwR0REOn2iS4NswBOPzJ7I6kKsRV6o3WOEuoQOuEi-4o`, the released hash, obtained with `network_seed: 'Epistemic Resonance-0.3.x'`; the bootstrap server for that space went from **one agent to two**; each node lists the other as a peer; the node's `storageArc` settled at **`[0, 4294967295]` — a full arc, holding the whole space**, about a minute after joining; and `systemctl --user restart` brings it back unattended on the same key and the same cell. **THE TRAP THIS LEAVES, and it is the silent kind:** the seed carries the DESKTOP'S MINOR VERSION, so a `0.4.0` release moves the live network to `Epistemic Resonance-0.4.x` while this node goes on serving `0.3.x` in perfect health — an always-on node on an abandoned DHT, indistinguishable from a working one by every check above except the first. `verify.mjs` carries that warning beside the constant; **nothing enforces it**, and a gate for it would be a real row. **AND THE DISCLOSURE WAS DELIVERED THE SAME DAY, WHICH IS WHAT CLOSES THIS ROW.** A full-arc node of ours now stores every claim anyone publishes, continuously, and **no participant has been told**. `docs/the-invitation.md` discloses permanence and says nothing about storage — 7.16's own complaint about that file, now with a machine behind it. Saying so cost nothing: the file now opens with FIVE obliged-to-tell items rather than four, the new one placed after the permanence pair because it answers what permanence raises — *on whose machine?* — and naming the only remedy that is real, which is that the node can be turned OFF and cannot be made to selectively forget, since wiping it re-gossips everything back. **An offer to delete your copy would be false comfort and is not made.** **AND BOTH CURRENT PARTICIPANTS WERE TOLD THE SAME EVENING, IN THE DOCUMENT'S OWN WORDS** — they joined before the paragraph existed, so a document nobody re-reads would not have been disclosure to them. ---

## What this list does not contain

Anything whose blocker could not be stated. Two things were considered and left
out for that reason: a "speed and scalability" item, which `READINESS` already
says comes after real reports from step 4 and therefore has step 4 as its
blocker rather than a technical one; and a general "make it autopoietic" item,
which is §2 with the ambition removed — **the honest target is operational
closure in Varela's weaker sense, or Luhmann's autopoiesis of communications,
and not biological autopoiesis.** Varela himself declined to extend the term to
social systems. Self-production of the boundary and self-repair both stay out of
reach: a person still installs the hApp, and a system that cannot delete cannot
heal, only accrete and lose standing. **Coral reef rather than cell** — durable
accretion with living tissue on the surface — is the better description and the
better story.
